<SUBAGENT-STOP>
If you were dispatched as a subagent to execute a specific task, ignore this skill.
</SUBAGENT-STOP>

<EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
If you think there is even a 1% chance a skill might apply to what you are doing, you ABSOLUTELY MUST invoke the skill.

IF A SKILL APPLIES TO YOUR TASK, YOU DO NOT HAVE A CHOICE. YOU MUST USE IT.

This is not negotiable. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
</EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action** — including clarifying questions, exploring the codebase, or checking files. If it turns out wrong for the situation, you don't have to use it.

**Before entering plan mode:** if you haven't already brainstormed, invoke the brainstorming skill first.

Then announce "Using [skill] to [purpose]" and follow the skill exactly. If it has a checklist, create a todo per item.

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills apply, process skills come first — they set the approach, then implementation skills (frontend-design, etc.) carry it out. Brainstorming and systematic-debugging are Superpowers' most common process skills, but the rule holds for any of them.

- "Let's build X" → superpowers:brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
- "Fix this bug" → superpowers:systematic-debugging first, then domain skills.

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ≠ using the skill. Invoke it. |

# Ibex auto-DV fork — canonical agent instructions

This file is the single source of truth for AI agents in this repo. `AGENTS.md` (codex) delegates
here and mirrors only the Critical Invariants block below.

## Environment & flow

- `source ci/env.sh` first, always (login shell / `bash -lc`; see its header for the supported-shell
  contract). One-time setup and every verified build/run/coverage command: `docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md`.
- The UVM DV flow lives in `dv/uvm/core_ibex` (Makefile-driven; VCS via `SIMULATOR=vcs`); cosim
  architecture: `docs/dv/COSIM.md`; generated-test interface: `docs/dv/TB_CONTRACT.md` (once WS2 lands).

## DV principles

`docs/dv/dv_principles.md` binds all DV work, hand-written or generated. In one line: realistic
boundary stimulus, intent-derived checking that fails through a collected mechanism, honesty over
green, and the trust triad — TDD, mutation-proof, fcov-expectation — for every new test or checker.

## Cross-model review policy

- The executing model never self-approves.
- Before execution: the plan/spec/testplan is reviewed by the other model (Claude executes ⇒ codex
  reviews; codex executes ⇒ `claude -p` reviews). After execution: the diff is reviewed by the other
  model the same way.
- Review artifacts are committed under `docs/dv/reviews/`, each with a reviewer-identity header (CLI
  version, model ID, reasoning setting) and an explicit review target (commit SHAs / diff scope).
- Verdicts are machine-readable — `APPROVE` / `APPROVE-WITH-CHANGES` / `REQUEST-CHANGES` — and
  gating: no progress past `REQUEST-CHANGES` without a recorded re-review.
- Disagreements with a recorded controller ruling go to the human owner, not back into the loop.
- The `cross-review` skill executes this policy; use it rather than ad-hoc invocations.

## Critical invariants

<!-- CRITICAL-INVARIANTS-BEGIN -->
1. The executing model never self-approves: plans get a pre-execution review and diffs get a
   post-execution review by the other model; a `REQUEST-CHANGES` verdict blocks progress until a
   recorded re-review reaches `APPROVE`/`APPROVE-WITH-CHANGES`.
2. Knowledge-fence: generation sessions must not read fenced DV collateral. Fence rules live in
   `docs/dv/FENCE.md` once WS7 lands; until a fence authority exists, generation sessions are
   NOT permitted at all (fail closed), and infra sessions never paste fenced content into
   allowed files.
<!-- CRITICAL-INVARIANTS-END -->

## Site gotchas

- The `module` command exits 1 even on success — never `&&`-chain it (`ci/env.sh` handles loads).
- Background/completion notifications are unreliable: poll log files and artifacts directly with
  deadlines, and put a watchdog timer on any dispatched work longer than ~5 minutes.
- Clear `PYTHONPATH` around pip operations (`~/.bashrc` leaks package metadata into `pip freeze`).
- Stale `metadata.pickle`: use `make clean` or a fresh `OUT=` after editing testlists/configs.
- Full gotcha list with evidence: `docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md`.

## Skills index

Skills live in `.claude/skills/` (codex discovers them via `.agents/skills/shared`). Agents live in
`.claude/agents/`. Index (updated as skills land): `cross-review`, `sim-debug`, `regress`,
`lint-check`, `dv-principles-check`, `mutation-check`, `fcov-expectation`, `simple-english`.
Review rubrics (model-neutral prompt files): `ci/reviews/`.

# dv-principles-check

Input: a diff range or file list. This check reviews ONLY conformance to
`docs/dv/dv_principles.md` (read it fresh each run — never quote this skill as the source; the
doc is the single source of truth).

Procedure: for each changed DV artifact, walk the doc's sections and cite violations as
`[§N bullet] file:line — what violates it — the conforming alternative`. Pay specific attention
to: §1 boundary-realistic stimulus and randomize-don't-walk; §2 fail-through-a-collected-mechanism
and derive-from-intent; §4 don't-hide-failures; §5 comment/single-source rules; §6 the trust triad
(TDD evidence, mutation-proof, fcov-expectation — for any new test/checker, absence of triad
evidence is itself a finding).

Output: PASS, or findings list + a one-line verdict. Scope discipline: general quality/bug review
belongs to the review rubrics (`ci/reviews/`), not here; flag only principle conformance.

codex

## Subagent dispatch requires multi-agent support

Add to your Codex config (`~/.codex/config.toml`):

```toml
[features]
multi_agent = true
```

This enables the multi-agent tools that skills like
`dispatching-parallel-agents` and `subagent-driven-development` use.
Which tools you get depends on the multi-agent version your model
preset selects (current presets run V2; older ones run V1). Trust your
actual tool list over any table — including this one — when they
disagree.

- **Spawning:** give children a clean context with
  `spawn_agent {fork_turns: "none"}`; the default `"all"` copies your
  entire transcript into the child. On Codex 0.145+, role files under
  `~/.codex/agents/` attach to isolated forks via `agent_type`.
  Full-history forks accept `model` and `reasoning_effort` overrides
  (only `agent_type` is refused there) — isolated forks are the SDD
  default for context hygiene, not because overrides require them.
- **Fix rounds:** resume the implementer with `followup_task` — it
  delivers your message, triggers a turn, and transparently reloads a
  child the harness evicted. Never dispatch a fresh implementer on the
  theory that a spawned agent cannot be messaged again; on V2 it
  always can.
- **Lifecycle:** V2 has no `close_agent`. Finished children are
  evicted automatically when slots are needed; leaving them unclosed
  costs nothing. Only V1 sessions have `close_agent` — there, close
  reviewers when their review returns, and close each implementer
  after its task's review passes.
- **Model names:** never copy a model name from a skill, table, or old
  session into `spawn_agent` without checking it against your current
  spawn allowlist — V2 accepts only V2-capable presets and hard-errors
  on the rest.

## Waiting on children

`wait_agent` is an event subscription, not a poll: a long wait wakes
the moment a child produces mailbox activity, with the same latency as
a short one. Short-timeout polling buys nothing and costs a tool call —
and a context rebill — per poll. In measured sessions, roughly
two-thirds of all wait calls were short polls that timed out.

- While you still have local work, do not wait at all. A completed
  child's final answer is pushed into your mailbox and arrives with
  your next turn.
- When you are genuinely idle with children outstanding, wait in
  bounded stretches: `wait_agent` with `timeout_ms` 300000-600000
  (5-10 minutes). After each stretch — wake or timeout — post one
  status line, run `list_agents`, and chase any child that finished
  without reporting. Never stack polls shorter than five minutes; the
  event subscription wakes a bounded stretch just as fast as a short
  one.
- Completion mail cannot wake an idle controller (it is delivered
  without triggering a turn); covering that idle window is
  `wait_agent`'s only job. A stretch that times out with no activity
  is your cue to reconcile, not to shorten the next stretch.

## Model routing on spawns

Every `spawn_agent` you issue — including when you are yourself a
spawned child running a fan-out — sets `model` AND `reasoning_effort`
explicitly, per the Model Selection rules of the skill you are
executing. Setting `model` alone is a trap: the child's effort
silently resets to that model's default, not to yours.

Ask your human partner to add a machine-level backstop to
`~/.codex/config.toml` so any spawn that slips through still routes to
a deliberate tier instead of silently inheriting the session's most
expensive model:

```toml
[agents]
default_subagent_model = "<a mid-tier model from your spawn allowlist>"
default_subagent_reasoning_effort = "medium"
```

## Environment Detection

Skills that create worktrees or finish branches should detect their
environment with read-only git commands before proceeding:

```bash
GIT_DIR=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
GIT_COMMON=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)
```

- `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` → already in a linked worktree (skip creation)
- `BRANCH` empty → detached HEAD (cannot branch/push/PR from sandbox)

See `using-git-worktrees` Step 0 and `finishing-a-development-branch`
Step 1 for how each skill uses these signals.

## Codex App Finishing

When the sandbox blocks branch/push operations (detached HEAD in an
externally managed worktree), the agent commits all work and informs
the user to use the App's native controls:

- **"Create branch"** — names the branch, then commit/push/PR via App UI
- **"Hand off to local"** — transfers work to the user's local checkout

The agent can still run tests, stage files, and output suggested branch
names, commit messages, and PR descriptions for the user to copy.

     1	# Building and Running Ibex DV Simulations (VCS)
     2	
     3	This document covers the `core_ibex` UVM DV flow (`dv/uvm/core_ibex`) under
     4	Synopsys VCS with Spike as the co-simulator, on this site's tooling. Every
     5	command below was actually run during WS1 bring-up; wall-times are what was
     6	observed on this host (a 192-core machine), not vendor/upstream estimates.
     7	
     8	## One-time setup
     9	
    10	Run once per checkout (or whenever the tools move):
    11	
    12	If you are not fzhang, first `export IBEX_TOOLS_DIR=/path/to/writable/dir` —
    13	it defaults to `/localdev/fzhang/ws/tools`.
    14	
    15	```bash
    16	source ci/env.sh
    17	bash ci/setup-venv.sh
    18	bash ci/build-spike.sh
    19	bash ci/get-toolchain.sh
    20	```
    21	
    22	- `ci/env.sh` is the single source of truth for tool paths: it loads the
    23	  site's VCS/licenses/dtc Modules (see Gotchas below), and exports
    24	  `IBEX_PYTHON`, `RISCV_GCC`/`RISCV_TOOLCHAIN`, `SPIKE_PATH`, and the venv's
    25	  `PKG_CONFIG_PATH`. Source it in every new shell before running anything
    26	  else in this document.
    27	- `ci/setup-venv.sh` creates `.venv/` and installs from `ci/requirements.lock`
    28	  when present, falling back to `python-requirements.txt` (including
    29	  riscv-dv's own requirements) otherwise, via `$IBEX_PYTHON`. Idempotent —
    30	  safe to re-run.
    31	- `ci/build-spike.sh` clones and builds the lowRISC fork of Spike (pinned
    32	  revision) and installs it to `$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/spike-ibex-cosim`. Requires
    33	  `dtc` on PATH, which `ci/env.sh` loads via Modules. Observed: ~51s wall
    34	  time on this host.
    35	- `ci/get-toolchain.sh` downloads and unpacks the lowRISC
    36	  `rv32imcb` prebuilt GCC toolchain to
    37	  `$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/lowrisc-toolchain-gcc-rv32imcb`. Observed: ~1s to
    38	  download the 41.8M tarball on this host's network; idempotent (skips the
    39	  download if the tarball is already present).
    40	
    41	## Running a single test
    42	
    43	```bash
    44	source ci/env.sh
    45	cd dv/uvm/core_ibex
    46	make clean
    47	make SIMULATOR=vcs IBEX_CONFIG=opentitan ISS=spike \
    48	     TEST=riscv_arithmetic_basic_test ITERATIONS=1 SEED=1
    49	```
    50	
    51	Observed: **~72s total wall time** on this host for a fully fresh build
    52	(TB compile ~39s of that; the flow's own once-off estimates for this class
    53	of build are much larger — 30-60 min — but do not hold on this host). A
    54	second from-scratch run during doc verification took ~156s instead (same
    55	~39s TB compile, extra time in env/module-load overhead) — expect some
    56	run-to-run variance on this host, not just this one number.
    57	
    58	This one `make` invocation runs the whole flow: riscv-dv instruction
    59	generation, cross-compile to a binary, VCS testbench build, and the VCS +
    60	Spike cosim run. Results land under `out/`:
    61	
    62	- `out/run/regr.log` — pass/fail summary (`100.00% PASS 1 PASSED, 0 FAILED`
    63	  on success).
    64	- `out/run/report.html` / `out/run/report.json` / `out/run/regr_junit.xml` —
    65	  same result in HTML/JSON/JUnit form.
    66	- `out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/` — one directory per test/seed, containing
    67	  `test.bin`, `rtl_sim_stdstreams.log` (see Gotchas), `trace_core_*.log`
    68	  (ibex RTL trace), and `spike_cosim_trace_core_*.log` (Spike reference
    69	  trace).
    70	- `out/build/tb/compile_tb_stdstreams.log` — VCS testbench compile log.
    71	- `out/build/instr_gen/build_stdout.log` — riscv-dv generator VCS build log.
    72	
    73	## Running a regression
    74	
    75	Same `make` invocation, varying `TEST`:
    76	
    77	- `TEST=all` (the Makefile default) — every riscv-dv **and** directed test
    78	  in both testlists.
    79	- `TEST=all_riscvdv` — only tests from `riscv_dv_extension/testlist.yaml`.
    80	- `TEST=all_directed` — only tests from
    81	  `directed_tests/directed_testlist.yaml`.
    82	- `TEST=test_a,test_b` — a comma-separated list of specific test names (mix
    83	  riscv-dv and directed names freely; `all`/`all_riscvdv`/`all_directed` can
    84	  also appear as list entries).
    85	
    86	`ITERATIONS=N` overrides each selected test's iteration count from its
    87	testlist entry. `SEED=S` is the *starting* seed: for a test run with `N`
    88	iterations, seeds `S, S+1, ..., S+N-1` are used, one per iteration
    89	(`out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/` per iteration). If `SEED` is omitted, the
    90	Makefile picks a random starting seed (`$RANDOM`) — pin `SEED` explicitly
    91	for anything you need to reproduce.
    92	
    93	## Coverage
    94	
    95	```bash
    96	source ci/env.sh
    97	cd dv/uvm/core_ibex
    98	make SIMULATOR=vcs IBEX_CONFIG=opentitan ISS=spike \
    99	     TEST=riscv_arithmetic_basic_test ITERATIONS=1 SEED=1 COV=1 OUT=out_cov2
   100	```
   101	
   102	Result: `100.00% PASS 1 PASSED, 0 FAILED`, urg overall score `52.13`
   103	(LINE 70.46, TOGGLE 39.37, FSM 20.93, BRANCH 62.52, ASSERT 85.63,
   104	GROUP 33.86). This run compiles in the FCIBH workaround (see Gotchas) — it
   105	is not a plain vanilla VCS build.
   106	
   107	Artifacts (under `$OUT/run/coverage/`):
   108	
   109	- `merged.vdb` — the urg-merged coverage database.
   110	- `report/` — the urg HTML+text report (`dashboard.txt`/`dashboard.html`,
   111	  `groups.*`, `asserts.*`).
   112	- `fcov/` — riscv-dv's own functional-coverage analysis
   113	  (`test.vdb` + `sim_riscv_instr_cov_test_0_0.log`).
   114	
   115	Full evidence, including the FCIBH root-cause and fix, is in
   116	`docs/dv/evidence/ws1-cov-summary.txt`.
   117	
   118	**Waiver caveat:** `dv/uvm/core_ibex/waivers/aux_code.vRefine` and
   119	`.../unr.vRefine` exist on disk but are **not** referenced by
   120	`scripts/merge_cov.py` or any other script — coverage waivers are not
   121	applied to the urg merge automatically. Load them manually in Verdi/urg if
   122	you need waived coverage numbers.
   123	
   124	## Configs
   125	
   126	`IBEX_CONFIG` selects one of 8 named configs from the top-level
   127	`ibex_configs.yaml`. The DV Makefile's default is `opentitan`
   128	(`dv/uvm/core_ibex/Makefile`); the top-level RTL build Makefile's default is
   129	`small` (`Makefile`) — pass `IBEX_CONFIG` explicitly if you need one
   130	consistently across both.
   131	
   132	| Config | Notes |
   133	|---|---|
   134	| `small` | 2-stage pipeline, no branch-target ALU, 3-cycle mul, no ICache/PMP/SecureIbex. `BaseIsa=RV32I`, `RV32ZC=Zca`. |
   135	| `opentitan` | Matches the OpenTitan project's Ibex integration. `BaseIsa=RV32IorCHERIoT`, ICache+ECC+scramble, SecureIbex, PMP (16 regions), DbgTriggerEn. DV Makefile default. |
   136	| `maxperf` | 3-stage pipeline, branch-target ALU, 1-cycle mul, no ICache/PMP. Max performance ignoring the (unverified) branch predictor. |
   137	| `maxperf-pmp` | `maxperf` + PMP enabled, 16 regions. |
   138	| `maxperf-pmp-bmbalanced` | `maxperf` + PMP + balanced bitmanip (`RV32BBalanced`). |
   139	| `maxperf-pmp-bmfull` | `maxperf-pmp` + full bitmanip (`RV32BFull`). |
   140	| `maxperf-pmp-bmfull-icache` | `maxperf-pmp-bmfull` + ICache/ICacheECC enabled. |
   141	| `experimental-branch-predictor` | `maxperf`-style config with `BranchPredictor=1` — experimental, not fully verified. |
   142	
   143	Each riscv-dv/directed testlist entry may carry an `rtl_params` map (e.g.
   144	`SecureIbex: 1`); `scripts/ibex_cmd.py:filter_tests_by_config()` drops any
   145	test whose `rtl_params` aren't all satisfied by the selected config before
   146	building the regression's test list — e.g. `SecureIbex`-gated tests are
   147	silently excluded from a `small` or `maxperf` run, not failed.
   148	
   149	## Gotchas
   150	
   151	- **Stale `metadata.pickle`.** After editing a testlist or
   152	  `ibex_configs.yaml`, `metadata.py` refuses to recreate metadata if
   153	  `out/metadata/metadata.pickle` already exists from a prior run. Use
   154	  `make clean` or a fresh `OUT=<new_dir>` after such edits.
   155	- **riscv-dv generator is VCS-compiled too.** The instruction generator
   156	  (`out/build/instr_gen/`) is its own separate VCS build, distinct from the
   157	  TB build (`out/build/tb/`) — a fresh regression run consumes two VCS
   158	  builds and two VCS licenses, not one.
   159	- **Site `module` command exits 1 even on success.** This site's Modules
   160	  install prints `Module ERROR: invalid command name "module-hide"` Tcl
   161	  noise on every load (harmless) and returns exit status 1 regardless of
   162	  whether the load succeeded. Never `&&`-chain a `module load`;
   163	  `ci/env.sh` already accounts for this.
   164	- **VCS `-l` quirk: sim stdout lands in `rtl_sim_stdstreams.log`,** not
   165	  `rtl_sim.log` (the latter also exists and duplicates the same content).
   166	  Similarly: TB compile log is
   167	  `out/build/tb/compile_tb_stdstreams.log`; the riscv-dv generator build
   168	  log is `out/build/instr_gen/build_stdout.log`; the per-test
   169	  cross-compile log is `out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/compile.riscvdv.log`
   170	  (0 bytes on a clean compile — benign).
   171	- **`.vRefine` waivers are not applied automatically** — see Coverage
   172	  section above.
   173	- **`WAVES=1` needs `$VERDI_HOME` for FSDB waveforms** (`vcs.tcl`); without
   174	  it, VCS falls back to VPD (`waves.vpd` instead of `waves.fsdb`).
   175	- **FCIBH workaround (VCS-only).** VCS half-implements SystemVerilog's
   176	  `default sequence` for transition-bin coverage: under `COV=1`,
   177	  `core_ibex_fcov_if.sv`'s `cp_controller_fsm`/`cp_controller_fsm_sleep`
   178	  covergroups fatally errored (`Error-[FCIBH] Illegal bin hit`) on legal FSM
   179	  self-loops (e.g. `RESET=>RESET`) because VCS treats every unlisted
   180	  transition as illegal rather than the empty set upstream intended. Fixed
   181	  by guarding the `illegal_bins ... = default sequence;` blocks behind
   182	  `` `ifndef FCOV_NO_DEFAULT_SEQUENCE ``, with that macro defined only in
   183	  the `vcs` tool's compile command in
   184	  `dv/uvm/core_ibex/yaml/rtl_simulation.yaml` (commit `dbba358f`) — no other
   185	  simulator's build is affected. A real fix requires enumerating the FSM's
   186	  legal transitions explicitly instead of relying on `default sequence`;
   187	  out of scope here.
   188	- **`IBEX_CFG_*` define-name mismatch (fixed, commit `54e01775`).** The TB
   189	  (`dv/uvm/core_ibex/tb/core_ibex_tb_top.sv`) previously guarded on
   190	  `IBEX_CFG_BASE_ISA`/`IBEX_CFG_REG_FILE` while `util/ibex_config.py` emits
   191	  `+define+IBEX_CFG_BaseIsa`/`+define+IBEX_CFG_RegFile` — the spelling
   192	  mismatch meant the `` `ifdef ``s never matched, so every config silently
   193	  elaborated with the TB's own CHERIoT-capable defaults regardless of
   194	  `IBEX_CONFIG`. `RV32ZC` had no TB forwarding at all. All three are fixed;
   195	  the TB now prints a `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=... RegFile=... RV32ZC=...`
   196	  banner at time 0 as permanent runtime evidence that a given config
   197	  actually reached elaboration — e.g. `opentitan` prints
   198	  `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=BaseIsaRV32IorCHERIoT RegFile=RegFileFF
   199	  RV32ZC=RV32ZcaZcbZcmp`, `small` prints
   200	  `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=BaseIsaRV32I RegFile=RegFileFF RV32ZC=RV32Zca`
   201	  (see `docs/dv/evidence/ws1-smoke-regr-note.txt`).
   202	- **Direct `scripts/*.py` invocation needs `PYTHONPATH`.** Running any
   203	  `dv/uvm/core_ibex/scripts/*.py` script directly (bypassing `make`) needs
   204	  `PYTHONPATH` set via `scripts.setup_imports.get_pythonpath()` first — the
   205	  Makefile does this automatically for every target; a bare
   206	  `python3 scripts/metadata.py --help` will fail with
   207	  `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'ibex_config'` otherwise.

ibex env: vcs=/tools_vendor/synopsys/vcs/X-2025.06-SP2/bin/vcs gcc=/localdev/fzhang/ws/tools/lowrisc-toolchain-gcc-rv32imcb/bin/riscv32-unknown-elf-gcc spike=/localdev/fzhang/ws/tools/spike-ibex-cosim
     1	# Ibex DV principles
     2	
     3	Recurring verification best-practices for this repo — the centralized "how we verify" reference
     4	for humans and agents. It binds hand-written and generated DV equally: tests, TB components,
     5	checkers, coverage, and the tooling around them. Flow commands live in
     6	[`BUILD_AND_SIM.md`](BUILD_AND_SIM.md); cosim architecture in [`COSIM.md`](COSIM.md); the
     7	generated-test interface contract in `TB_CONTRACT.md` (once WS2 lands).
     8	
     9	## 1. Stimulus & drive
    10	
    11	- **Realistic drive at the DUT boundary.** Drive ibex the way a real system does: instructions
    12	  through generated programs (riscv-dv or directed assembly) fetched over the instruction bus,
    13	  data through the memory interface agents, interrupts through the irq lines. A backdoor or
    14	  force is acceptable only where the boundary itself is the thing being modeled (e.g. memory
    15	  model contents) — never as a shortcut around a DUT input you could drive for real. A forced
    16	  internal net can silently no-op under VCS visibility rules; if a backdoor is unavoidable,
    17	  verify it took (write → clock → read back → error on mismatch).
    18	- **If it's a config, program it.** CSR-controlled behavior is set through real CSR-writing
    19	  instructions in the test program, not by poking DUT state. TB-parameter behavior is set
    20	  through the config system (`IBEX_CONFIG`, `+define+IBEX_CFG_*`) — and the `TB-CONFIG:` banner
    21	  exists so every log proves which configuration actually elaborated.
    22	- **Randomize, don't walk.** A test explores its state space to catch corners, not to confirm
    23	  one path. Randomly select stimulus per iteration with distribution weights; pin a directed
    24	  iteration inside the random stream only to guarantee a specific corner. Never reduce a test
    25	  to a single directed case "because the answer is known".
    26	- **Future-proof every count.** Derive counts and ranges from their canonical parameter
    27	  (`ibex_pkg` enums/parameters, config fields), never a re-typed literal — when the design
    28	  parameter changes, the stimulus must follow with no edit.
    29	- **Cover the transition, not just the steady state.** Interleave alternative modes (e.g.
    30	  interrupt on/off phases, debug entries, PMP region reconfiguration) so the switch itself is
    31	  exercised, not just each mode in isolation.
    32	- **Own your preconditions.** A test programs the machine state its behavior depends on
    33	  (privilege mode, MIE/MSTATUS, PMP setup) explicitly at entry and assumes nothing about
    34	  incoming state; on observing unexpected state, error rather than adapt silently.
    35	- **Sensitizing operands.** Distinct, non-degenerate data; interpretation-distinct values so a
    36	  wrong interpretation is observable. No all-zeros or identity values that mask a fault.
    37	
    38	## 2. Checking & correctness
    39	
    40	- **Check at the interface boundary; internal signals only when unavoidable.** Prefer checks at
    41	  module/bus boundaries and architectural state (the cosim comparison is the model: retired
    42	  instructions, RF writes, PC). An internal-signal check is fragile to design change and does
    43	  not show end-to-end behavior — when one is unavoidable, make its failure message identify
    44	  itself ("checked signal moved/renamed", not a phantom bug) and pair it with an end-to-end
    45	  check.
    46	- **Deterministic ⇒ derive from intent.** A reference model computes expected values from the
    47	  ISA/spec and the driven stimulus — never by copying an RTL decode net. A model mirrored from
    48	  the RTL only proves the RTL matches itself. Reach for an internal anchor only when the
    49	  quantity's starting state is genuinely unknowable from intent, and document it.
    50	- **End-to-end round-trip.** Use data again through the consumer's output (store then load
    51	  back; CSR write then read). Handing data off correctly does not prove it was applied.
    52	- **Place the check where the failure lands — never before it.** A check that passes before
    53	  full propagation is a false pass waiting to happen; gate on the commit/settle event.
    54	- **Verify the result of every transaction — including the "harmless" ones.** A read issued
    55	  only to observe state must still assert both that it landed and that the value matches; a
    56	  dropped or perturbing read otherwise passes unnoticed.
    57	- **Fail through a mechanism the flow actually collects.** In the SV-UVM flow, that is
    58	  `uvm_error`/`uvm_fatal` (scanned by `scripts/check_logs.py` into `trr.yaml`/`regr.log`); in
    59	  cocotb tests, a Python `assert`/raised exception fails the cocotb test which fails the run. A
    60	  bare `$display`/log line fails nothing. Know your failure path and prove it once (see §6).
    61	- **Predict-and-check.** Per-feature expectations computed from randomized stimulus + sampled
    62	  config, compared against observation. Keep predictors pure and host-testable where possible.
    63	- **Ground-truth at the waveform for new checkers.** Confirm a new monitor/checker against the
    64	  actual wave at the cycles of interest at least once — it catches mis-reads (wrong hierarchical
    65	  handle, packed-signal misinterpretation, silent-idle None handle) that a green test hides.
    66	
    67	## 3. Scoreboard & infrastructure
    68	
    69	- **Passive, always-on checking is the default.** The best check is one a test author cannot
    70	  forget: derived automatically, applied on every transaction in every test (the cosim referee
    71	  is the archetype). Active in-test checks exist for (a) "did the targeted scenario actually
    72	  fire" — the reason the test exists — and (b) checks unique or too costly to run passively.
    73	  Both layers are needed; a passive floor does not remove a test's duty to check its own
    74	  driven data and effects.
    75	- **Always-true invariants live in shared infrastructure; "did it fire" lives in the test.**
    76	- **No-modify reuse of shared/vendored infra.** riscv-dv and lowrisc_ip are vendored: extend
    77	  through the extension points (`riscv_dv_extension/`, TB hooks), don't patch vendored code
    78	  ad-hoc. When a genuine vendor patch is required it goes through `vendor/patches/` with the
    79	  vendoring flow, never a direct edit.
    80	
    81	## 4. RTL truth & honesty
    82	
    83	- **Ground in intent, log divergence.** Models and testplans derive from the ISA spec and
    84	  documented intent. When RTL and intent disagree: if the RTL is more complete/correct, RTL
    85	  wins and the disagreement is logged; if the RTL is less complete than the documented intent,
    86	  that is a latent-bug candidate — write the test to the intended behavior, mark it as a known
    87	  expected-fail, and drive the fix. DV does not modify RTL to make tests pass.
    88	- **Don't hide failures.** Never fake-pass, silently down-scope, or paper over something that
    89	  cannot be driven or checked; surface limitations explicitly (the FCIBH workaround in
    90	  `BUILD_AND_SIM.md` is the pattern: documented, scoped, with the follow-up named).
    91	- **Evidence over inference.** If a question is answerable from waves, RTL, docs, git history,
    92	  or a quick experiment — check it. State unverified claims as unverified. A mechanism you
    93	  explained but did not observe is a guess.
    94	- **FCOV: build samplable, prune impossible.** A coverpoint may be built before stimulus can
    95	  hit it (0% marks intent), but genuinely unhittable bins are pruned/`ignore_bins`'d so
    96	  coverage isn't diluted. No duplicate coverpoints — extend, don't re-cover. Beware simulator
    97	  semantics: `illegal_bins = default sequence` is not portable (see the FCIBH gotcha).
    98	
    99	## 5. Tooling & hygiene
   100	
   101	- **Toggleable debug, never unconditional prints.** Debug output gates behind a plusarg;
   102	  plusarg names are declared once (test/TB constants), not scattered string literals — a
   103	  mistyped gate silently no-ops.
   104	- **Concise diagnostics.** A failure message states its triggering condition — id plus
   105	  expected-vs-actual — nothing more. Root-cause narration belongs in docs, not runtime messages.
   106	- **Self-sufficient docs and code.** Every doc, commit message, comment, and identifier is
   107	  resolvable on its own: expand a tag/ID on first use or link the defining doc. Code never
   108	  depends on a bare cross-file positional index or opaque tag; bind by named import or stable
   109	  key that fails loud on reorder.
   110	- **Concise code comments — intent only.** What and why, never history ("moved from X",
   111	  "previously Y") — that lives in git.
   112	- **Single source of truth.** A value meaning the same thing in two places is defined once and
   113	  imported: `ibex_pkg` parameters, config fields, one constants home per language domain.
   114	- **No hardcoded paths — filesystem or hierarchy.** Filesystem paths anchor to the repo root
   115	  or `ci/env.sh`-exported variables. Hierarchical RTL references (SV bind paths, cocotb
   116	  handles) resolve through one central place per domain (the fcov binds; the cocotb common
   117	  package), so a rename fails loudly there instead of silently per call-site.
   118	- **Site realities** (see `BUILD_AND_SIM.md` gotchas): the module command exits nonzero on
   119	  success; background notifications are unreliable — poll artifacts with deadlines and watchdog
   120	  long-running work; clear `PYTHONPATH` around pip operations.
   121	
   122	## 6. Trust & evidence
   123	
   124	Every claim about verification is backed by committed evidence — logs, reports, banners — not
   125	prose. The `TB-CONFIG:` banner, the evidence files under `docs/dv/evidence/`, and the review
   126	artifacts under `docs/dv/reviews/` are the standing examples.
   127	
   128	<!-- TRUST-TRIAD-CANONICAL-BEGIN -->
   129	**The trust triad** — required for every new test, checker, assertion, or covergroup, whether
   130	human-written or generated:
   131	
   132	1. **TDD** — the behavior is specified by a failing check before the implementation that makes
   133	   it pass; the red→green transcript is part of the evidence.
   134	2. **Mutation-proof** — a new checker or assertion counts only when a named mutation (recorded
   135	   as id, file:line, original, mutated, expected detector) is caught by the NAMED checker with
   136	   hidden referees inert, plus a checker-ablation negative control (checker disabled ⇒ the
   137	   mutation survives). "Hidden referees inert" operationally: run with `+disable_cosim=1` (cosim
   138	   mismatches become informational and cannot fail the test) and verify the failure signature in
   139	   the log belongs to the named checker.
   140	3. **fcov-expectation** — every new test declares the functional-coverage bins it intends to
   141	   hit; declared-but-unhit bins FAIL the run. Verification is per-test and pre-merge (merged
   142	   databases let one test claim another's bins), generated covergroups live in an isolated
   143	   namespace, and every sampling condition gets an anti-vacuity review (a bin hit by an
   144	   always-true sample proves nothing).
   145	<!-- TRUST-TRIAD-CANONICAL-END -->
   146	
   147	- **Self-proving checks.** The triad's rules 2 and 3 generalize: a check never exercised to
   148	  fail is not trusted; a stimulus never proven to arrive is not trusted.
   149	- **Reviewer independence.** The executing model never self-approves — the cross-model review
   150	  policy in `CLAUDE.md` governs; artifacts live under `docs/dv/reviews/`.
   151	- **No hidden-referee reliance.** Generated DV must carry its own checking and may not lean on
   152	  referees it cannot see (the cosim demotion rules and the checking obligation live in the
   153	  spec's fence workstream and `TB_CONTRACT.md` once WS2 lands).

    25	--------------------------------------------------------------------
    26	Fix history: FCIBH illegal-bin-hit workaround
    27	--------------------------------------------------------------------
    28	The first COV=1 attempt (OUT=out_cov) FAILED the test: VCS raised a
    29	fatal coverage "VERIFICATION ERROR" and aborted simulation after only
    30	2064350 ps:
    31	
    32	  Error-[FCIBH] Illegal bin hit
    33	  dv/uvm/core_ibex/fcov/core_ibex_fcov_if.sv, 588 (covergroup core_ibex_fcov_if::uarch_cg)
    34	  Illegal transition bin illegal_transitions of coverpoint cp_controller_fsm
    35	  got hit with transition RESET=>RESET
    36	
    37	Cause: `illegal_bins illegal_transitions = default sequence;` on
    38	cp_controller_fsm / cp_controller_fsm_sleep relies on SystemVerilog's
    39	`default sequence`, which VCS does not implement (the source already
    40	carried an in-source TODO to this effect since 2022, commit aee235cfa).
    41	VCS instead treats every unlisted transition — including legal
    42	self-loops like RESET=>RESET — as an illegal-bin hit, which is fatal.
    43	This only surfaces once the fcov covergroups are built/sampled, i.e.
    44	only under COV=1 (Task 6's COV=0 run never enabled +enable_ibex_fcov or
    45	-cm and so never hit it).
    46	
    47	Fix (VCS-only, gated by a compile-time macro so no other simulator's
    48	build is affected):
    49	
    50	  dv/uvm/core_ibex/fcov/core_ibex_fcov_if.sv
    51	    Both `illegal_bins illegal_transitions = default sequence;` blocks
    52	    (cp_controller_fsm, cp_controller_fsm_sleep) wrapped in
    53	    `` `ifndef FCOV_NO_DEFAULT_SEQUENCE `` / `` `endif ``.
    54	
    55	  dv/uvm/core_ibex/yaml/rtl_simulation.yaml
    56	    `+define+FCOV_NO_DEFAULT_SEQUENCE` added to the vcs tool's compile
    57	    cmd only (no other simulator entry touched).
    58	
    59	Confirmed clean of FCIBH hits on the final rerun too
    60	(`grep -c "FCIBH" out_ws1_cov/run/tests/riscv_arithmetic_basic_test.1/rtl_sim_stdstreams.log`
    61	== 0, see ws1-manifest.txt for the run this was checked against).
    62	
    63	Step 3 — vRefine waiver caveat (design-time finding, independent of the

    48	</SUBAGENT-STOP>

    51	If you think there is even a 1% chance a skill might apply to what you are doing, you ABSOLUTELY MUST invoke the skill.
    52

    54	
    55	This is not negotiable. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
    56	</EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
    57	
    58	## The Rule
    59	
    60	**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action** — including clarifying questions, exploring the codebase, or checking files. If it turns out wrong for the situation, you don't have to use it.
    61	
    62	**Before entering plan mode:** if you haven't already brainstormed, invoke the brainstorming skill first.
    63	
    64	Then announce "Using [skill] to [purpose]" and follow the skill exactly. If it has a checklist, create a todo per item.
    65	
    66	## Skill Priority
    67	
    68	When multiple skills apply, process skills come first — they set the approach, then implementation skills (frontend-design, etc.) carry it out. Brainstorming and systematic-debugging are Superpowers' most common process skills, but the rule holds for any of them.
    69	
    70	- "Let's build X" → superpowers:brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
    71	- "Fix this bug" → superpowers:systematic-debugging first, then domain skills.
    72	
    73	## Red Flags
    74	
    75	These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:
    76	
    77	| Thought | Reality |
    78	|---------|---------|
    79	| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
    80	| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
    81	| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
    82	| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
    83	| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
    84	| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
    85	| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
    86	| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
    87	| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
    88	| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
    89	| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
    90	| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ≠ using the skill. Invoke it. |

   117	# Ibex auto-DV fork — canonical agent instructions
   118	
   119	This file is the single source of truth for AI agents in this repo. `AGENTS.md` (codex) delegates
   120	here and mirrors only the Critical Invariants block below.
   121	
   122	## Environment & flow
   123	
   124	- `source ci/env.sh` first, always (login shell / `bash -lc`; see its header for the supported-shell
   125	  contract). One-time setup and every verified build/run/coverage command: `docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md`.
   126	- The UVM DV flow lives in `dv/uvm/core_ibex` (Makefile-driven; VCS via `SIMULATOR=vcs`); cosim
   127	  architecture: `docs/dv/COSIM.md`; generated-test interface: `docs/dv/TB_CONTRACT.md` (once WS2 lands).
   128	
   129	## DV principles
   130	
   131	`docs/dv/dv_principles.md` binds all DV work, hand-written or generated. In one line: realistic
   132	boundary stimulus, intent-derived checking that fails through a collected mechanism, honesty over
   133	green, and the trust triad — TDD, mutation-proof, fcov-expectation — for every new test or checker.
   134	
   135	## Cross-model review policy
   136	
   137	- The executing model never self-approves.
   138	- Before execution: the plan/spec/testplan is reviewed by the other model (Claude executes ⇒ codex
   139	  reviews; codex executes ⇒ `claude -p` reviews). After execution: the diff is reviewed by the other
   140	  model the same way.
   141	- Review artifacts are committed under `docs/dv/reviews/`, each with a reviewer-identity header (CLI
   142	  version, model ID, reasoning setting) and an explicit review target (commit SHAs / diff scope).
   143	- Verdicts are machine-readable — `APPROVE` / `APPROVE-WITH-CHANGES` / `REQUEST-CHANGES` — and
   144	  gating: no progress past `REQUEST-CHANGES` without a recorded re-review.
   145	- Disagreements with a recorded controller ruling go to the human owner, not back into the loop.
   146	- The `cross-review` skill executes this policy; use it rather than ad-hoc invocations.
   147	
   148	## Critical invariants
   149	
   150	<!-- CRITICAL-INVARIANTS-BEGIN -->
   151	1. The executing model never self-approves: plans get a pre-execution review and diffs get a
   152	   post-execution review by the other model; a `REQUEST-CHANGES` verdict blocks progress until a
   153	   recorded re-review reaches `APPROVE`/`APPROVE-WITH-CHANGES`.
   154	2. Knowledge-fence: generation sessions must not read fenced DV collateral. Fence rules live in
   155	   `docs/dv/FENCE.md` once WS7 lands; until a fence authority exists, generation sessions are
   156	   NOT permitted at all (fail closed), and infra sessions never paste fenced content into
   157	   allowed files.
   158	<!-- CRITICAL-INVARIANTS-END -->
   159	
   160	## Site gotchas
   161	
   162	- The `module` command exits 1 even on success — never `&&`-chain it (`ci/env.sh` handles loads).
   163	- Background/completion notifications are unreliable: poll log files and artifacts directly with
   164	  deadlines, and put a watchdog timer on any dispatched work longer than ~5 minutes.
   165	- Clear `PYTHONPATH` around pip operations (`~/.bashrc` leaks package metadata into `pip freeze`).
   166	- Stale `metadata.pickle`: use `make clean` or a fresh `OUT=` after editing testlists/configs.
   167	- Full gotcha list with evidence: `docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md`.
   168

   173	`lint-check`, `dv-principles-check`, `mutation-check`, `fcov-expectation`, `simple-english`.
   174	Review rubrics (model-neutral prompt files): `ci/reviews/`.

   233	## Subagent dispatch requires multi-agent support
   234	
   235	Add to your Codex config (`~/.codex/config.toml`):
   236	
   237	```toml
   238	[features]
   239	multi_agent = true
   240	```
   241	
   242	This enables the multi-agent tools that skills like
   243	`dispatching-parallel-agents` and `subagent-driven-development` use.
   244	Which tools you get depends on the multi-agent version your model
   245	preset selects (current presets run V2; older ones run V1). Trust your
   246	actual tool list over any table — including this one — when they
   247	disagree.
   248	
   249	- **Spawning:** give children a clean context with
   250	  `spawn_agent {fork_turns: "none"}`; the default `"all"` copies your
   251	  entire transcript into the child. On Codex 0.145+, role files under
   252	  `~/.codex/agents/` attach to isolated forks via `agent_type`.
   253	  Full-history forks accept `model` and `reasoning_effort` overrides
   254	  (only `agent_type` is refused there) — isolated forks are the SDD
   255	  default for context hygiene, not because overrides require them.
   256	- **Fix rounds:** resume the implementer with `followup_task` — it
   257	  delivers your message, triggers a turn, and transparently reloads a
   258	  child the harness evicted. Never dispatch a fresh implementer on the
   259	  theory that a spawned agent cannot be messaged again; on V2 it
   260	  always can.
   261	- **Lifecycle:** V2 has no `close_agent`. Finished children are
   262	  evicted automatically when slots are needed; leaving them unclosed
   263	  costs nothing. Only V1 sessions have `close_agent` — there, close
   264	  reviewers when their review returns, and close each implementer
   265	  after its task's review passes.
   266	- **Model names:** never copy a model name from a skill, table, or old
   267	  session into `spawn_agent` without checking it against your current
   268	  spawn allowlist — V2 accepts only V2-capable presets and hard-errors
   269	  on the rest.
   270	
   271	## Waiting on children
   272	
   273	`wait_agent` is an event subscription, not a poll: a long wait wakes
   274	the moment a child produces mailbox activity, with the same latency as
   275	a short one. Short-timeout polling buys nothing and costs a tool call —
   276	and a context rebill — per poll. In measured sessions, roughly
   277	two-thirds of all wait calls were short polls that timed out.
   278	
   279	- While you still have local work, do not wait at all. A completed
   280	  child's final answer is pushed into your mailbox and arrives with
   281	  your next turn.
   282	- When you are genuinely idle with children outstanding, wait in
   283	  bounded stretches: `wait_agent` with `timeout_ms` 300000-600000
   284	  (5-10 minutes). After each stretch — wake or timeout — post one
   285	  status line, run `list_agents`, and chase any child that finished
   286	  without reporting. Never stack polls shorter than five minutes; the
   287	  event subscription wakes a bounded stretch just as fast as a short
   288	  one.
   289	- Completion mail cannot wake an idle controller (it is delivered
   290	  without triggering a turn); covering that idle window is
   291	  `wait_agent`'s only job. A stretch that times out with no activity
   292	  is your cue to reconcile, not to shorten the next stretch.
   293	
   294	## Model routing on spawns
   295	
   296	Every `spawn_agent` you issue — including when you are yourself a
   297	spawned child running a fan-out — sets `model` AND `reasoning_effort`
   298	explicitly, per the Model Selection rules of the skill you are
   299	executing. Setting `model` alone is a trap: the child's effort
   300	silently resets to that model's default, not to yours.
   301	
   302	Ask your human partner to add a machine-level backstop to
   303	`~/.codex/config.toml` so any spawn that slips through still routes to
   304	a deliberate tier instead of silently inheriting the session's most
   305	expensive model:
   306	
   307	```toml
   308	[agents]
   309	default_subagent_model = "<a mid-tier model from your spawn allowlist>"
   310	default_subagent_reasoning_effort = "medium"
   311	```
   312	
   313	## Environment Detection
   314	
   315	Skills that create worktrees or finish branches should detect their
   316	environment with read-only git commands before proceeding:
   317	
   318	```bash
   319	GIT_DIR=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
   320	GIT_COMMON=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
   321	BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)
   322	```
   323	
   324	- `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` → already in a linked worktree (skip creation)
   325	- `BRANCH` empty → detached HEAD (cannot branch/push/PR from sandbox)
   326	
   327	See `using-git-worktrees` Step 0 and `finishing-a-development-branch`
   328	Step 1 for how each skill uses these signals.
   329	
   330	## Codex App Finishing
   331	
   332	When the sandbox blocks branch/push operations (detached HEAD in an
   333	externally managed worktree), the agent commits all work and informs
   334	the user to use the App's native controls:
   335	
   336	- **"Create branch"** — names the branch, then commit/push/PR via App UI
   337	- **"Hand off to local"** — transfers work to the user's local checkout
   338	
   339	The agent can still run tests, stage files, and output suggested branch
   340	names, commit messages, and PR descriptions for the user to copy.
   341

   355	     1	# Building and Running Ibex DV Simulations (VCS)
   356	     2	
   357	     3	This document covers the `core_ibex` UVM DV flow (`dv/uvm/core_ibex`) under
   358	     4	Synopsys VCS with Spike as the co-simulator, on this site's tooling. Every
   359	     5	command below was actually run during WS1 bring-up; wall-times are what was
   360	     6	observed on this host (a 192-core machine), not vendor/upstream estimates.
   361	     7	
   362	     8	## One-time setup
   363	     9	
   364	    10	Run once per checkout (or whenever the tools move):
   365	    11	
   366	    12	If you are not fzhang, first `export IBEX_TOOLS_DIR=/path/to/writable/dir` —
   367	    13	it defaults to `/localdev/fzhang/ws/tools`.
   368	    14	
   369	    15	```bash
   370	    16	source ci/env.sh
   371	    17	bash ci/setup-venv.sh
   372	    18	bash ci/build-spike.sh
   373	    19	bash ci/get-toolchain.sh
   374	    20	```
   375	    21	
   376	    22	- `ci/env.sh` is the single source of truth for tool paths: it loads the
   377	    23	  site's VCS/licenses/dtc Modules (see Gotchas below), and exports
   378	    24	  `IBEX_PYTHON`, `RISCV_GCC`/`RISCV_TOOLCHAIN`, `SPIKE_PATH`, and the venv's
   379	    25	  `PKG_CONFIG_PATH`. Source it in every new shell before running anything
   380	    26	  else in this document.
   381	    27	- `ci/setup-venv.sh` creates `.venv/` and installs from `ci/requirements.lock`
   382	    28	  when present, falling back to `python-requirements.txt` (including
   383	    29	  riscv-dv's own requirements) otherwise, via `$IBEX_PYTHON`. Idempotent —
   384	    30	  safe to re-run.
   385	    31	- `ci/build-spike.sh` clones and builds the lowRISC fork of Spike (pinned
   386	    32	  revision) and installs it to `$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/spike-ibex-cosim`. Requires
   387	    33	  `dtc` on PATH, which `ci/env.sh` loads via Modules. Observed: ~51s wall
   388	    34	  time on this host.
   389	    35	- `ci/get-toolchain.sh` downloads and unpacks the lowRISC
   390	    36	  `rv32imcb` prebuilt GCC toolchain to
   391	    37	  `$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/lowrisc-toolchain-gcc-rv32imcb`. Observed: ~1s to
   392	    38	  download the 41.8M tarball on this host's network; idempotent (skips the
   393	    39	  download if the tarball is already present).
   394	    40	
   395	    41	## Running a single test
   396	    42	
   397	    43	```bash
   398	    44	source ci/env.sh
   399	    45	cd dv/uvm/core_ibex
   400	    46	make clean
   401	    47	make SIMULATOR=vcs IBEX_CONFIG=opentitan ISS=spike \
   402	    48	     TEST=riscv_arithmetic_basic_test ITERATIONS=1 SEED=1
   403	    49	```
   404	    50	
   405	    51	Observed: **~72s total wall time** on this host for a fully fresh build
   406	    52	(TB compile ~39s of that; the flow's own once-off estimates for this class
   407	    53	of build are much larger — 30-60 min — but do not hold on this host). A
   408	    54	second from-scratch run during doc verification took ~156s instead (same
   409	    55	~39s TB compile, extra time in env/module-load overhead) — expect some
   410	    56	run-to-run variance on this host, not just this one number.
   411	    57	
   412	    58	This one `make` invocation runs the whole flow: riscv-dv instruction
   413	    59	generation, cross-compile to a binary, VCS testbench build, and the VCS +
   414	    60	Spike cosim run. Results land under `out/`:
   415	    61	
   416	    62	- `out/run/regr.log` — pass/fail summary (`100.00% PASS 1 PASSED, 0 FAILED`
   417	    63	  on success).
   418	    64	- `out/run/report.html` / `out/run/report.json` / `out/run/regr_junit.xml` —
   419	    65	  same result in HTML/JSON/JUnit form.
   420	    66	- `out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/` — one directory per test/seed, containing
   421	    67	  `test.bin`, `rtl_sim_stdstreams.log` (see Gotchas), `trace_core_*.log`
   422	    68	  (ibex RTL trace), and `spike_cosim_trace_core_*.log` (Spike reference
   423	    69	  trace).
   424	    70	- `out/build/tb/compile_tb_stdstreams.log` — VCS testbench compile log.
   425	    71	- `out/build/instr_gen/build_stdout.log` — riscv-dv generator VCS build log.
   426	    72	
   427	    73	## Running a regression
   428	    74	
   429	    75	Same `make` invocation, varying `TEST`:
   430	    76	
   431	    77	- `TEST=all` (the Makefile default) — every riscv-dv **and** directed test
   432	    78	  in both testlists.
   433	    79	- `TEST=all_riscvdv` — only tests from `riscv_dv_extension/testlist.yaml`.
   434	    80	- `TEST=all_directed` — only tests from
   435	    81	  `directed_tests/directed_testlist.yaml`.
   436	    82	- `TEST=test_a,test_b` — a comma-separated list of specific test names (mix
   437	    83	  riscv-dv and directed names freely; `all`/`all_riscvdv`/`all_directed` can
   438	    84	  also appear as list entries).
   439	    85	
   440	    86	`ITERATIONS=N` overrides each selected test's iteration count from its
   441	    87	testlist entry. `SEED=S` is the *starting* seed: for a test run with `N`
   442	    88	iterations, seeds `S, S+1, ..., S+N-1` are used, one per iteration
   443	    89	(`out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/` per iteration). If `SEED` is omitted, the
   444	    90	Makefile picks a random starting seed (`$RANDOM`) — pin `SEED` explicitly
   445	    91	for anything you need to reproduce.
   446	    92	
   447	    93	## Coverage
   448	    94	
   449	    95	```bash
   450	    96	source ci/env.sh
   451	    97	cd dv/uvm/core_ibex
   452	    98	make SIMULATOR=vcs IBEX_CONFIG=opentitan ISS=spike \
   453	    99	     TEST=riscv_arithmetic_basic_test ITERATIONS=1 SEED=1 COV=1 OUT=out_cov2
   454	   100	```
   455	   101	
   456	   102	Result: `100.00% PASS 1 PASSED, 0 FAILED`, urg overall score `52.13`
   457	   103	(LINE 70.46, TOGGLE 39.37, FSM 20.93, BRANCH 62.52, ASSERT 85.63,
   458	   104	GROUP 33.86). This run compiles in the FCIBH workaround (see Gotchas) — it
   459	   105	is not a plain vanilla VCS build.
   460	   106	
   461	   107	Artifacts (under `$OUT/run/coverage/`):
   462	   108	
   463	   109	- `merged.vdb` — the urg-merged coverage database.
   464	   110	- `report/` — the urg HTML+text report (`dashboard.txt`/`dashboard.html`,
   465	   111	  `groups.*`, `asserts.*`).
   466	   112	- `fcov/` — riscv-dv's own functional-coverage analysis
   467	   113	  (`test.vdb` + `sim_riscv_instr_cov_test_0_0.log`).
   468	   114	
   469	   115	Full evidence, including the FCIBH root-cause and fix, is in
   470	   116	`docs/dv/evidence/ws1-cov-summary.txt`.
   471	   117	
   472	   118	**Waiver caveat:** `dv/uvm/core_ibex/waivers/aux_code.vRefine` and
   473	   119	`.../unr.vRefine` exist on disk but are **not** referenced by
   474	   120	`scripts/merge_cov.py` or any other script — coverage waivers are not
   475	   121	applied to the urg merge automatically. Load them manually in Verdi/urg if
   476	   122	you need waived coverage numbers.
   477	   123	
   478	   124	## Configs
   479	   125	
   480	   126	`IBEX_CONFIG` selects one of 8 named configs from the top-level
   481	   127	`ibex_configs.yaml`. The DV Makefile's default is `opentitan`
   482	   128	(`dv/uvm/core_ibex/Makefile`); the top-level RTL build Makefile's default is
   483	   129	`small` (`Makefile`) — pass `IBEX_CONFIG` explicitly if you need one
   484	   130	consistently across both.
   485	   131	
   486	   132	| Config | Notes |
   487	   133	|---|---|
   488	   134	| `small` | 2-stage pipeline, no branch-target ALU, 3-cycle mul, no ICache/PMP/SecureIbex. `BaseIsa=RV32I`, `RV32ZC=Zca`. |
   489	   135	| `opentitan` | Matches the OpenTitan project's Ibex integration. `BaseIsa=RV32IorCHERIoT`, ICache+ECC+scramble, SecureIbex, PMP (16 regions), DbgTriggerEn. DV Makefile default. |
   490	   136	| `maxperf` | 3-stage pipeline, branch-target ALU, 1-cycle mul, no ICache/PMP. Max performance ignoring the (unverified) branch predictor. |
   491	   137	| `maxperf-pmp` | `maxperf` + PMP enabled, 16 regions. |
   492	   138	| `maxperf-pmp-bmbalanced` | `maxperf` + PMP + balanced bitmanip (`RV32BBalanced`). |
   493	   139	| `maxperf-pmp-bmfull` | `maxperf-pmp` + full bitmanip (`RV32BFull`). |
   494	   140	| `maxperf-pmp-bmfull-icache` | `maxperf-pmp-bmfull` + ICache/ICacheECC enabled. |
   495	   141	| `experimental-branch-predictor` | `maxperf`-style config with `BranchPredictor=1` — experimental, not fully verified. |
   496	   142	
   497	   143	Each riscv-dv/directed testlist entry may carry an `rtl_params` map (e.g.
   498	   144	`SecureIbex: 1`); `scripts/ibex_cmd.py:filter_tests_by_config()` drops any
   499	   145	test whose `rtl_params` aren't all satisfied by the selected config before
   500	   146	building the regression's test list — e.g. `SecureIbex`-gated tests are
   501	   147	silently excluded from a `small` or `maxperf` run, not failed.
   502	   148	
   503	   149	## Gotchas
   504	   150	
   505	   151	- **Stale `metadata.pickle`.** After editing a testlist or
   506	   152	  `ibex_configs.yaml`, `metadata.py` refuses to recreate metadata if
   507	   153	  `out/metadata/metadata.pickle` already exists from a prior run. Use
   508	   154	  `make clean` or a fresh `OUT=<new_dir>` after such edits.
   509	   155	- **riscv-dv generator is VCS-compiled too.** The instruction generator
   510	   156	  (`out/build/instr_gen/`) is its own separate VCS build, distinct from the
   511	   157	  TB build (`out/build/tb/`) — a fresh regression run consumes two VCS
   512	   158	  builds and two VCS licenses, not one.
   513	   159	- **Site `module` command exits 1 even on success.** This site's Modules
   514	   160	  install prints `Module ERROR: invalid command name "module-hide"` Tcl
   515	   161	  noise on every load (harmless) and returns exit status 1 regardless of
   516	   162	  whether the load succeeded. Never `&&`-chain a `module load`;
   517	   163	  `ci/env.sh` already accounts for this.
   518	   164	- **VCS `-l` quirk: sim stdout lands in `rtl_sim_stdstreams.log`,** not
   519	   165	  `rtl_sim.log` (the latter also exists and duplicates the same content).
   520	   166	  Similarly: TB compile log is
   521	   167	  `out/build/tb/compile_tb_stdstreams.log`; the riscv-dv generator build
   522	   168	  log is `out/build/instr_gen/build_stdout.log`; the per-test
   523	   169	  cross-compile log is `out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/compile.riscvdv.log`
   524	   170	  (0 bytes on a clean compile — benign).
   525	   171	- **`.vRefine` waivers are not applied automatically** — see Coverage
   526	   172	  section above.
   527	   173	- **`WAVES=1` needs `$VERDI_HOME` for FSDB waveforms** (`vcs.tcl`); without
   528	   174	  it, VCS falls back to VPD (`waves.vpd` instead of `waves.fsdb`).
   529	   175	- **FCIBH workaround (VCS-only).** VCS half-implements SystemVerilog's
   530	   176	  `default sequence` for transition-bin coverage: under `COV=1`,
   531	   177	  `core_ibex_fcov_if.sv`'s `cp_controller_fsm`/`cp_controller_fsm_sleep`
   532	   178	  covergroups fatally errored (`Error-[FCIBH] Illegal bin hit`) on legal FSM
   533	   179	  self-loops (e.g. `RESET=>RESET`) because VCS treats every unlisted
   534	   180	  transition as illegal rather than the empty set upstream intended. Fixed
   535	   181	  by guarding the `illegal_bins ... = default sequence;` blocks behind
   536	   182	  `` `ifndef FCOV_NO_DEFAULT_SEQUENCE ``, with that macro defined only in
   537	   183	  the `vcs` tool's compile command in
   538	   184	  `dv/uvm/core_ibex/yaml/rtl_simulation.yaml` (commit `dbba358f`) — no other
   539	   185	  simulator's build is affected. A real fix requires enumerating the FSM's
   540	   186	  legal transitions explicitly instead of relying on `default sequence`;
   541	   187	  out of scope here.
   542	   188	- **`IBEX_CFG_*` define-name mismatch (fixed, commit `54e01775`).** The TB
   543	   189	  (`dv/uvm/core_ibex/tb/core_ibex_tb_top.sv`) previously guarded on
   544	   190	  `IBEX_CFG_BASE_ISA`/`IBEX_CFG_REG_FILE` while `util/ibex_config.py` emits
   545	   191	  `+define+IBEX_CFG_BaseIsa`/`+define+IBEX_CFG_RegFile` — the spelling
   546	   192	  mismatch meant the `` `ifdef ``s never matched, so every config silently
   547	   193	  elaborated with the TB's own CHERIoT-capable defaults regardless of
   548	   194	  `IBEX_CONFIG`. `RV32ZC` had no TB forwarding at all. All three are fixed;
   549	   195	  the TB now prints a `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=... RegFile=... RV32ZC=...`
   550	   196	  banner at time 0 as permanent runtime evidence that a given config
   551	   197	  actually reached elaboration — e.g. `opentitan` prints
   552	   198	  `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=BaseIsaRV32IorCHERIoT RegFile=RegFileFF
   553	   199	  RV32ZC=RV32ZcaZcbZcmp`, `small` prints
   554	   200	  `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=BaseIsaRV32I RegFile=RegFileFF RV32ZC=RV32Zca`
   555	   201	  (see `docs/dv/evidence/ws1-smoke-regr-note.txt`).
   556	   202	- **Direct `scripts/*.py` invocation needs `PYTHONPATH`.** Running any
   557	   203	  `dv/uvm/core_ibex/scripts/*.py` script directly (bypassing `make`) needs
   558	   204	  `PYTHONPATH` set via `scripts.setup_imports.get_pythonpath()` first — the
   559	   205	  Makefile does this automatically for every target; a bare
   560	   206	  `python3 scripts/metadata.py --help` will fail with
   561	   207	  `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'ibex_config'` otherwise.

   576	     1	# Ibex DV principles
   577	     2	
   578	     3	Recurring verification best-practices for this repo — the centralized "how we verify" reference
   579	     4	for humans and agents. It binds hand-written and generated DV equally: tests, TB components,
   580	     5	checkers, coverage, and the tooling around them. Flow commands live in
   581	     6	[`BUILD_AND_SIM.md`](BUILD_AND_SIM.md); cosim architecture in [`COSIM.md`](COSIM.md); the
   582	     7	generated-test interface contract in `TB_CONTRACT.md` (once WS2 lands).
   583	     8	
   584	     9	## 1. Stimulus & drive
   585	    10	
   586	    11	- **Realistic drive at the DUT boundary.** Drive ibex the way a real system does: instructions
   587	    12	  through generated programs (riscv-dv or directed assembly) fetched over the instruction bus,
   588	    13	  data through the memory interface agents, interrupts through the irq lines. A backdoor or
   589	    14	  force is acceptable only where the boundary itself is the thing being modeled (e.g. memory
   590	    15	  model contents) — never as a shortcut around a DUT input you could drive for real. A forced
   591	    16	  internal net can silently no-op under VCS visibility rules; if a backdoor is unavoidable,
   592	    17	  verify it took (write → clock → read back → error on mismatch).
   593	    18	- **If it's a config, program it.** CSR-controlled behavior is set through real CSR-writing
   594	    19	  instructions in the test program, not by poking DUT state. TB-parameter behavior is set
   595	    20	  through the config system (`IBEX_CONFIG`, `+define+IBEX_CFG_*`) — and the `TB-CONFIG:` banner
   596	    21	  exists so every log proves which configuration actually elaborated.
   597	    22	- **Randomize, don't walk.** A test explores its state space to catch corners, not to confirm
   598	    23	  one path. Randomly select stimulus per iteration with distribution weights; pin a directed
   599	    24	  iteration inside the random stream only to guarantee a specific corner. Never reduce a test
   600	    25	  to a single directed case "because the answer is known".
   601	    26	- **Future-proof every count.** Derive counts and ranges from their canonical parameter
   602	    27	  (`ibex_pkg` enums/parameters, config fields), never a re-typed literal — when the design
   603	    28	  parameter changes, the stimulus must follow with no edit.
   604	    29	- **Cover the transition, not just the steady state.** Interleave alternative modes (e.g.
   605	    30	  interrupt on/off phases, debug entries, PMP region reconfiguration) so the switch itself is
   606	    31	  exercised, not just each mode in isolation.
   607	    32	- **Own your preconditions.** A test programs the machine state its behavior depends on
   608	    33	  (privilege mode, MIE/MSTATUS, PMP setup) explicitly at entry and assumes nothing about
   609	    34	  incoming state; on observing unexpected state, error rather than adapt silently.
   610	    35	- **Sensitizing operands.** Distinct, non-degenerate data; interpretation-distinct values so a
   611	    36	  wrong interpretation is observable. No all-zeros or identity values that mask a fault.
   612	    37	
   613	    38	## 2. Checking & correctness
   614	    39	
   615	    40	- **Check at the interface boundary; internal signals only when unavoidable.** Prefer checks at
   616	    41	  module/bus boundaries and architectural state (the cosim comparison is the model: retired
   617	    42	  instructions, RF writes, PC). An internal-signal check is fragile to design change and does
   618	    43	  not show end-to-end behavior — when one is unavoidable, make its failure message identify
   619	    44	  itself ("checked signal moved/renamed", not a phantom bug) and pair it with an end-to-end
   620	    45	  check.
   621	    46	- **Deterministic ⇒ derive from intent.** A reference model computes expected values from the
   622	    47	  ISA/spec and the driven stimulus — never by copying an RTL decode net. A model mirrored from
   623	    48	  the RTL only proves the RTL matches itself. Reach for an internal anchor only when the
   624	    49	  quantity's starting state is genuinely unknowable from intent, and document it.
   625	    50	- **End-to-end round-trip.** Use data again through the consumer's output (store then load
   626	    51	  back; CSR write then read). Handing data off correctly does not prove it was applied.
   627	    52	- **Place the check where the failure lands — never before it.** A check that passes before
   628	    53	  full propagation is a false pass waiting to happen; gate on the commit/settle event.
   629	    54	- **Verify the result of every transaction — including the "harmless" ones.** A read issued
   630	    55	  only to observe state must still assert both that it landed and that the value matches; a
   631	    56	  dropped or perturbing read otherwise passes unnoticed.
   632	    57	- **Fail through a mechanism the flow actually collects.** In the SV-UVM flow, that is
   633	    58	  `uvm_error`/`uvm_fatal` (scanned by `scripts/check_logs.py` into `trr.yaml`/`regr.log`); in
   634	    59	  cocotb tests, a Python `assert`/raised exception fails the cocotb test which fails the run. A
   635	    60	  bare `$display`/log line fails nothing. Know your failure path and prove it once (see §6).
   636	    61	- **Predict-and-check.** Per-feature expectations computed from randomized stimulus + sampled
   637	    62	  config, compared against observation. Keep predictors pure and host-testable where possible.
   638	    63	- **Ground-truth at the waveform for new checkers.** Confirm a new monitor/checker against the
   639	    64	  actual wave at the cycles of interest at least once — it catches mis-reads (wrong hierarchical
   640	    65	  handle, packed-signal misinterpretation, silent-idle None handle) that a green test hides.
   641	    66	
   642	    67	## 3. Scoreboard & infrastructure
   643	    68	
   644	    69	- **Passive, always-on checking is the default.** The best check is one a test author cannot
   645	    70	  forget: derived automatically, applied on every transaction in every test (the cosim referee
   646	    71	  is the archetype). Active in-test checks exist for (a) "did the targeted scenario actually
   647	    72	  fire" — the reason the test exists — and (b) checks unique or too costly to run passively.
   648	    73	  Both layers are needed; a passive floor does not remove a test's duty to check its own
   649	    74	  driven data and effects.
   650	    75	- **Always-true invariants live in shared infrastructure; "did it fire" lives in the test.**
   651	    76	- **No-modify reuse of shared/vendored infra.** riscv-dv and lowrisc_ip are vendored: extend
   652	    77	  through the extension points (`riscv_dv_extension/`, TB hooks), don't patch vendored code
   653	    78	  ad-hoc. When a genuine vendor patch is required it goes through `vendor/patches/` with the
   654	    79	  vendoring flow, never a direct edit.
   655	    80	
   656	    81	## 4. RTL truth & honesty
   657	    82	
   658	    83	- **Ground in intent, log divergence.** Models and testplans derive from the ISA spec and
   659	    84	  documented intent. When RTL and intent disagree: if the RTL is more complete/correct, RTL
   660	    85	  wins and the disagreement is logged; if the RTL is less complete than the documented intent,
   661	    86	  that is a latent-bug candidate — write the test to the intended behavior, mark it as a known
   662	    87	  expected-fail, and drive the fix. DV does not modify RTL to make tests pass.
   663	    88	- **Don't hide failures.** Never fake-pass, silently down-scope, or paper over something that
   664	    89	  cannot be driven or checked; surface limitations explicitly (the FCIBH workaround in
   665	    90	  `BUILD_AND_SIM.md` is the pattern: documented, scoped, with the follow-up named).
   666	    91	- **Evidence over inference.** If a question is answerable from waves, RTL, docs, git history,
   667	    92	  or a quick experiment — check it. State unverified claims as unverified. A mechanism you
   668	    93	  explained but did not observe is a guess.
   669	    94	- **FCOV: build samplable, prune impossible.** A coverpoint may be built before stimulus can
   670	    95	  hit it (0% marks intent), but genuinely unhittable bins are pruned/`ignore_bins`'d so
   671	    96	  coverage isn't diluted. No duplicate coverpoints — extend, don't re-cover. Beware simulator
   672	    97	  semantics: `illegal_bins = default sequence` is not portable (see the FCIBH gotcha).
   673	    98	
   674	    99	## 5. Tooling & hygiene
   675	   100	
   676	   101	- **Toggleable debug, never unconditional prints.** Debug output gates behind a plusarg;
   677	   102	  plusarg names are declared once (test/TB constants), not scattered string literals — a
   678	   103	  mistyped gate silently no-ops.
   679	   104	- **Concise diagnostics.** A failure message states its triggering condition — id plus
   680	   105	  expected-vs-actual — nothing more. Root-cause narration belongs in docs, not runtime messages.
   681	   106	- **Self-sufficient docs and code.** Every doc, commit message, comment, and identifier is
   682	   107	  resolvable on its own: expand a tag/ID on first use or link the defining doc. Code never
   683	   108	  depends on a bare cross-file positional index or opaque tag; bind by named import or stable
   684	   109	  key that fails loud on reorder.
   685	   110	- **Concise code comments — intent only.** What and why, never history ("moved from X",
   686	   111	  "previously Y") — that lives in git.
   687	   112	- **Single source of truth.** A value meaning the same thing in two places is defined once and
   688	   113	  imported: `ibex_pkg` parameters, config fields, one constants home per language domain.
   689	   114	- **No hardcoded paths — filesystem or hierarchy.** Filesystem paths anchor to the repo root
   690	   115	  or `ci/env.sh`-exported variables. Hierarchical RTL references (SV bind paths, cocotb
   691	   116	  handles) resolve through one central place per domain (the fcov binds; the cocotb common
   692	   117	  package), so a rename fails loudly there instead of silently per call-site.
   693	   118	- **Site realities** (see `BUILD_AND_SIM.md` gotchas): the module command exits nonzero on
   694	   119	  success; background notifications are unreliable — poll artifacts with deadlines and watchdog
   695	   120	  long-running work; clear `PYTHONPATH` around pip operations.
   696	   121	
   697	   122	## 6. Trust & evidence
   698	   123	
   699	   124	Every claim about verification is backed by committed evidence — logs, reports, banners — not
   700	   125	prose. The `TB-CONFIG:` banner, the evidence files under `docs/dv/evidence/`, and the review
   701	   126	artifacts under `docs/dv/reviews/` are the standing examples.
   702	   127	
   703	   128	<!-- TRUST-TRIAD-CANONICAL-BEGIN -->
   704	   129	**The trust triad** — required for every new test, checker, assertion, or covergroup, whether
   705	   130	human-written or generated:
   706	   131	
   707	   132	1. **TDD** — the behavior is specified by a failing check before the implementation that makes
   708	   133	   it pass; the red→green transcript is part of the evidence.
   709	   134	2. **Mutation-proof** — a new checker or assertion counts only when a named mutation (recorded
   710	   135	   as id, file:line, original, mutated, expected detector) is caught by the NAMED checker with
   711	   136	   hidden referees inert, plus a checker-ablation negative control (checker disabled ⇒ the
   712	   137	   mutation survives). "Hidden referees inert" operationally: run with `+disable_cosim=1` (cosim
   713	   138	   mismatches become informational and cannot fail the test) and verify the failure signature in
   714	   139	   the log belongs to the named checker.
   715	   140	3. **fcov-expectation** — every new test declares the functional-coverage bins it intends to
   716	   141	   hit; declared-but-unhit bins FAIL the run. Verification is per-test and pre-merge (merged
   717	   142	   databases let one test claim another's bins), generated covergroups live in an isolated
   718	   143	   namespace, and every sampling condition gets an anti-vacuity review (a bin hit by an
   719	   144	   always-true sample proves nothing).
   720	   145	<!-- TRUST-TRIAD-CANONICAL-END -->
   721	   146	
   722	   147	- **Self-proving checks.** The triad's rules 2 and 3 generalize: a check never exercised to
   723	   148	  fail is not trusted; a stimulus never proven to arrive is not trusted.
   724	   149	- **Reviewer independence.** The executing model never self-approves — the cross-model review
   725	   150	  policy in `CLAUDE.md` governs; artifacts live under `docs/dv/reviews/`.
   726	   151	- **No hidden-referee reliance.** Generated DV must carry its own checking and may not lean on
   727	   152	  referees it cannot see (the cosim demotion rules and the checking obligation live in the
   728	   153	  spec's fence workstream and `TB_CONTRACT.md` once WS2 lands).
   729

  1021	    26	Fix history: FCIBH illegal-bin-hit workaround
  1022	    27	--------------------------------------------------------------------
  1023	    28	The first COV=1 attempt (OUT=out_cov) FAILED the test: VCS raised a
  1024	    29	fatal coverage "VERIFICATION ERROR" and aborted simulation after only
  1025	    30	2064350 ps:
  1026	    31	
  1027	    32	  Error-[FCIBH] Illegal bin hit
  1028	    33	  dv/uvm/core_ibex/fcov/core_ibex_fcov_if.sv, 588 (covergroup core_ibex_fcov_if::uarch_cg)
  1029	    34	  Illegal transition bin illegal_transitions of coverpoint cp_controller_fsm
  1030	    35	  got hit with transition RESET=>RESET
  1031	    36	
  1032	    37	Cause: `illegal_bins illegal_transitions = default sequence;` on
  1033	    38	cp_controller_fsm / cp_controller_fsm_sleep relies on SystemVerilog's
  1034	    39	`default sequence`, which VCS does not implement (the source already
  1035	    40	carried an in-source TODO to this effect since 2022, commit aee235cfa).
  1036	    41	VCS instead treats every unlisted transition — including legal
  1037	    42	self-loops like RESET=>RESET — as an illegal-bin hit, which is fatal.
  1038	    43	This only surfaces once the fcov covergroups are built/sampled, i.e.
  1039	    44	only under COV=1 (Task 6's COV=0 run never enabled +enable_ibex_fcov or
  1040	    45	-cm and so never hit it).
  1041	    46	
  1042	    47	Fix (VCS-only, gated by a compile-time macro so no other simulator's
  1043	    48	build is affected):
  1044	    49	
  1045	    50	  dv/uvm/core_ibex/fcov/core_ibex_fcov_if.sv
  1046	    51	    Both `illegal_bins illegal_transitions = default sequence;` blocks
  1047	    52	    (cp_controller_fsm, cp_controller_fsm_sleep) wrapped in
  1048	    53	    `` `ifndef FCOV_NO_DEFAULT_SEQUENCE `` / `` `endif ``.
  1049	    54	
  1050	    55	  dv/uvm/core_ibex/yaml/rtl_simulation.yaml
  1051	    56	    `+define+FCOV_NO_DEFAULT_SEQUENCE` added to the vcs tool's compile
  1052	    57	    cmd only (no other simulator entry touched).
  1053	    58	
  1054	    59	Confirmed clean of FCIBH hits on the final rerun too
  1055	    60	(`grep -c "FCIBH" out_ws1_cov/run/tests/riscv_arithmetic_basic_test.1/rtl_sim_stdstreams.log`
  1056	    61	== 0, see ws1-manifest.txt for the run this was checked against).
