    46	<SUBAGENT-STOP>
    47	If you were dispatched as a subagent to execute a specific task, ignore this skill.
    48	</SUBAGENT-STOP>
    49	
    50	<EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
    51	If you think there is even a 1% chance a skill might apply to what you are doing, you ABSOLUTELY MUST invoke the skill.
    52	
    53	IF A SKILL APPLIES TO YOUR TASK, YOU DO NOT HAVE A CHOICE. YOU MUST USE IT.
    54	
    55	This is not negotiable. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
    56	</EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
    57	
    58	## The Rule
    59	
    60	**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action** — including clarifying questions, exploring the codebase, or checking files. If it turns out wrong for the situation, you don't have to use it.
    61	
    62	**Before entering plan mode:** if you haven't already brainstormed, invoke the brainstorming skill first.
    63	
    64	Then announce "Using [skill] to [purpose]" and follow the skill exactly. If it has a checklist, create a todo per item.
    65	
    66	## Skill Priority
    67	
    68	When multiple skills apply, process skills come first — they set the approach, then implementation skills (frontend-design, etc.) carry it out. Brainstorming and systematic-debugging are Superpowers' most common process skills, but the rule holds for any of them.
    69	
    70	- "Let's build X" → superpowers:brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
    71	- "Fix this bug" → superpowers:systematic-debugging first, then domain skills.
    72	
    73	## Red Flags
    74	
    75	These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:
    76	
    77	| Thought | Reality |
    78	|---------|---------|
    79	| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
    80	| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
    81	| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
    82	| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
    83	| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
    84	| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
    85	| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
    86	| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
    87	| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
    88	| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
    89	| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
    90	| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ≠ using the skill. Invoke it. |
    91

   117	# Ibex auto-DV fork — canonical agent instructions
   118	
   119	This file is the single source of truth for AI agents in this repo. `AGENTS.md` (codex) delegates
   120	here and mirrors only the Critical Invariants block below.
   121	
   122	## Environment & flow
   123	
   124	- `source ci/env.sh` first, always (login shell / `bash -lc`; see its header for the supported-shell
   125	  contract). One-time setup and every verified build/run/coverage command: `docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md`.
   126	- The UVM DV flow lives in `dv/uvm/core_ibex` (Makefile-driven; VCS via `SIMULATOR=vcs`); cosim
   127	  architecture: `docs/dv/COSIM.md`; generated-test interface: `docs/dv/TB_CONTRACT.md` (once WS2 lands).
   128	
   129	## DV principles
   130	
   131	`docs/dv/dv_principles.md` binds all DV work, hand-written or generated. In one line: realistic
   132	boundary stimulus, intent-derived checking that fails through a collected mechanism, honesty over
   133	green, and the trust triad — TDD, mutation-proof, fcov-expectation — for every new test or checker.
   134	
   135	## Cross-model review policy
   136	
   137	- The executing model never self-approves.
   138	- Before execution: the plan/spec/testplan is reviewed by the other model (Claude executes ⇒ codex
   139	  reviews; codex executes ⇒ `claude -p` reviews). After execution: the diff is reviewed by the other
   140	  model the same way.
   141	- Review artifacts are committed under `docs/dv/reviews/`, each with a reviewer-identity header (CLI
   142	  version, model ID, reasoning setting) and an explicit review target (commit SHAs / diff scope).
   143	- Verdicts are machine-readable — `APPROVE` / `APPROVE-WITH-CHANGES` / `REQUEST-CHANGES` — and
   144	  gating: no progress past `REQUEST-CHANGES` without a recorded re-review.
   145	- Disagreements with a recorded controller ruling go to the human owner, not back into the loop.
   146	- The `cross-review` skill executes this policy; use it rather than ad-hoc invocations.
   147	
   148	## Critical invariants
   149	
   150	<!-- CRITICAL-INVARIANTS-BEGIN -->
   151	1. The executing model never self-approves: plans get a pre-execution review and diffs get a
   152	   post-execution review by the other model; a `REQUEST-CHANGES` verdict blocks progress until a
   153	   recorded re-review reaches `APPROVE`/`APPROVE-WITH-CHANGES`.
   154	2. Knowledge-fence: generation sessions must not read fenced DV collateral. Fence rules live in
   155	   `docs/dv/FENCE.md` once WS7 lands; until a fence authority exists, generation sessions are
   156	   NOT permitted at all (fail closed), and infra sessions never paste fenced content into
   157	   allowed files.
   158	<!-- CRITICAL-INVARIANTS-END -->
   159	
   160	## Site gotchas
   161	
   162	- The `module` command exits 1 even on success — never `&&`-chain it (`ci/env.sh` handles loads).
   163	- Background/completion notifications are unreliable: poll log files and artifacts directly with
   164	  deadlines, and put a watchdog timer on any dispatched work longer than ~5 minutes.
   165	- Clear `PYTHONPATH` around pip operations (`~/.bashrc` leaks package metadata into `pip freeze`).
   166	- Stale `metadata.pickle`: use `make clean` or a fresh `OUT=` after editing testlists/configs.
   167	- Full gotcha list with evidence: `docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md`.
   168	
   169	## Skills index
   170	
   171	Skills live in `.claude/skills/` (codex discovers them via `.agents/skills/shared`). Agents live in
   172	`.claude/agents/`. Index (updated as skills land): `cross-review`, `sim-debug`, `regress`,
   173	`lint-check`, `dv-principles-check`, `mutation-check`, `fcov-expectation`, `simple-english`.
   174	Review rubrics (model-neutral prompt files): `ci/reviews/`.

   193	# dv-principles-check
   194

   200	`[§N bullet] file:line — what violates it — the conforming alternative`. Pay specific attention
   201	to: §1 boundary-realistic stimulus and randomize-don't-walk; §2 fail-through-a-collected-mechanism
   202	and derive-from-intent; §4 don't-hide-failures; §5 comment/single-source rules; §6 the trust triad
   203	(TDD evidence, mutation-proof, fcov-expectation — for any new test/checker, absence of triad
   204	evidence is itself a finding).
   205	
   206	Output: PASS, or findings list + a one-line verdict. Scope discipline: general quality/bug review
   207	belongs to the review rubrics (`ci/reviews/`), not here; flag only principle conformance.

   233	## Subagent dispatch requires multi-agent support
   234	
   235	Add to your Codex config (`~/.codex/config.toml`):
   236	
   237	```toml
   238	[features]
   239	multi_agent = true
   240	```
   241	
   242	This enables the multi-agent tools that skills like
   243	`dispatching-parallel-agents` and `subagent-driven-development` use.
   244	Which tools you get depends on the multi-agent version your model
   245	preset selects (current presets run V2; older ones run V1). Trust your
   246	actual tool list over any table — including this one — when they
   247	disagree.
   248	
   249	- **Spawning:** give children a clean context with
   250	  `spawn_agent {fork_turns: "none"}`; the default `"all"` copies your
   251	  entire transcript into the child. On Codex 0.145+, role files under
   252	  `~/.codex/agents/` attach to isolated forks via `agent_type`.
   253	  Full-history forks accept `model` and `reasoning_effort` overrides
   254	  (only `agent_type` is refused there) — isolated forks are the SDD
   255	  default for context hygiene, not because overrides require them.
   256	- **Fix rounds:** resume the implementer with `followup_task` — it
   257	  delivers your message, triggers a turn, and transparently reloads a
   258	  child the harness evicted. Never dispatch a fresh implementer on the
   259	  theory that a spawned agent cannot be messaged again; on V2 it
   260	  always can.
   261	- **Lifecycle:** V2 has no `close_agent`. Finished children are
   262	  evicted automatically when slots are needed; leaving them unclosed
   263	  costs nothing. Only V1 sessions have `close_agent` — there, close
   264	  reviewers when their review returns, and close each implementer
   265	  after its task's review passes.
   266	- **Model names:** never copy a model name from a skill, table, or old
   267	  session into `spawn_agent` without checking it against your current
   268	  spawn allowlist — V2 accepts only V2-capable presets and hard-errors
   269	  on the rest.
   270	
   271	## Waiting on children
   272	
   273	`wait_agent` is an event subscription, not a poll: a long wait wakes
   274	the moment a child produces mailbox activity, with the same latency as
   275	a short one. Short-timeout polling buys nothing and costs a tool call —
   276	and a context rebill — per poll. In measured sessions, roughly
   277	two-thirds of all wait calls were short polls that timed out.
   278	
   279	- While you still have local work, do not wait at all. A completed
   280	  child's final answer is pushed into your mailbox and arrives with
   281	  your next turn.
   282	- When you are genuinely idle with children outstanding, wait in
   283	  bounded stretches: `wait_agent` with `timeout_ms` 300000-600000
   284	  (5-10 minutes). After each stretch — wake or timeout — post one
   285	  status line, run `list_agents`, and chase any child that finished
   286	  without reporting. Never stack polls shorter than five minutes; the
   287	  event subscription wakes a bounded stretch just as fast as a short
   288	  one.
   289	- Completion mail cannot wake an idle controller (it is delivered
   290	  without triggering a turn); covering that idle window is
   291	  `wait_agent`'s only job. A stretch that times out with no activity
   292	  is your cue to reconcile, not to shorten the next stretch.
   293	
   294	## Model routing on spawns
   295	
   296	Every `spawn_agent` you issue — including when you are yourself a
   297	spawned child running a fan-out — sets `model` AND `reasoning_effort`
   298	explicitly, per the Model Selection rules of the skill you are
   299	executing. Setting `model` alone is a trap: the child's effort
   300	silently resets to that model's default, not to yours.
   301	
   302	Ask your human partner to add a machine-level backstop to
   303	`~/.codex/config.toml` so any spawn that slips through still routes to
   304	a deliberate tier instead of silently inheriting the session's most
   305	expensive model:
   306	
   307	```toml
   308	[agents]
   309	default_subagent_model = "<a mid-tier model from your spawn allowlist>"
   310	default_subagent_reasoning_effort = "medium"
   311	```
   312	
   313	## Environment Detection
   314	
   315	Skills that create worktrees or finish branches should detect their
   316	environment with read-only git commands before proceeding:
   317	
   318	```bash
   319	GIT_DIR=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
   320	GIT_COMMON=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
   321	BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)
   322	```
   323	
   324	- `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` → already in a linked worktree (skip creation)
   325	- `BRANCH` empty → detached HEAD (cannot branch/push/PR from sandbox)
   326	
   327	See `using-git-worktrees` Step 0 and `finishing-a-development-branch`
   328	Step 1 for how each skill uses these signals.
   329	
   330	## Codex App Finishing
   331	
   332	When the sandbox blocks branch/push operations (detached HEAD in an
   333	externally managed worktree), the agent commits all work and informs
   334	the user to use the App's native controls:
   335	
   336	- **"Create branch"** — names the branch, then commit/push/PR via App UI
   337	- **"Hand off to local"** — transfers work to the user's local checkout
   338	
   339	The agent can still run tests, stage files, and output suggested branch
   340	names, commit messages, and PR descriptions for the user to copy.

   355	     1	# Building and Running Ibex DV Simulations (VCS)
   356	     2	
   357	     3	This document covers the `core_ibex` UVM DV flow (`dv/uvm/core_ibex`) under
   358	     4	Synopsys VCS with Spike as the co-simulator, on this site's tooling. Every
   359	     5	command below was actually run during WS1 bring-up; wall-times are what was
   360	     6	observed on this host (a 192-core machine), not vendor/upstream estimates.
   361	     7	
   362	     8	## One-time setup
   363	     9	
   364	    10	Run once per checkout (or whenever the tools move):
   365	    11	
   366	    12	If you are not fzhang, first `export IBEX_TOOLS_DIR=/path/to/writable/dir` —
   367	    13	it defaults to `/localdev/fzhang/ws/tools`.
   368	    14	
   369	    15	```bash
   370	    16	source ci/env.sh
   371	    17	bash ci/setup-venv.sh
   372	    18	bash ci/build-spike.sh
   373	    19	bash ci/get-toolchain.sh
   374	    20	```
   375	    21	
   376	    22	- `ci/env.sh` is the single source of truth for tool paths: it loads the
   377	    23	  site's VCS/licenses/dtc Modules (see Gotchas below), and exports
   378	    24	  `IBEX_PYTHON`, `RISCV_GCC`/`RISCV_TOOLCHAIN`, `SPIKE_PATH`, and the venv's
   379	    25	  `PKG_CONFIG_PATH`. Source it in every new shell before running anything
   380	    26	  else in this document.
   381	    27	- `ci/setup-venv.sh` creates `.venv/` and installs from `ci/requirements.lock`
   382	    28	  when present, falling back to `python-requirements.txt` (including
   383	    29	  riscv-dv's own requirements) otherwise, via `$IBEX_PYTHON`. Idempotent —
   384	    30	  safe to re-run.
   385	    31	- `ci/build-spike.sh` clones and builds the lowRISC fork of Spike (pinned
   386	    32	  revision) and installs it to `$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/spike-ibex-cosim`. Requires
   387	    33	  `dtc` on PATH, which `ci/env.sh` loads via Modules. Observed: ~51s wall
   388	    34	  time on this host.
   389	    35	- `ci/get-toolchain.sh` downloads and unpacks the lowRISC
   390	    36	  `rv32imcb` prebuilt GCC toolchain to
   391	    37	  `$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/lowrisc-toolchain-gcc-rv32imcb`. Observed: ~1s to
   392	    38	  download the 41.8M tarball on this host's network; idempotent (skips the
   393	    39	  download if the tarball is already present).
   394	    40	
   395	    41	## Running a single test
   396	    42	
   397	    43	```bash
   398	    44	source ci/env.sh
   399	    45	cd dv/uvm/core_ibex
   400	    46	make clean
   401	    47	make SIMULATOR=vcs IBEX_CONFIG=opentitan ISS=spike \
   402	    48	     TEST=riscv_arithmetic_basic_test ITERATIONS=1 SEED=1
   403	    49	```
   404	    50	
   405	    51	Observed: **~72s total wall time** on this host for a fully fresh build
   406	    52	(TB compile ~39s of that; the flow's own once-off estimates for this class
   407	    53	of build are much larger — 30-60 min — but do not hold on this host). A
   408	    54	second from-scratch run during doc verification took ~156s instead (same
   409	    55	~39s TB compile, extra time in env/module-load overhead) — expect some
   410	    56	run-to-run variance on this host, not just this one number.
   411	    57	
   412	    58	This one `make` invocation runs the whole flow: riscv-dv instruction
   413	    59	generation, cross-compile to a binary, VCS testbench build, and the VCS +
   414	    60	Spike cosim run. Results land under `out/`:
   415	    61	
   416	    62	- `out/run/regr.log` — pass/fail summary (`100.00% PASS 1 PASSED, 0 FAILED`
   417	    63	  on success).
   418	    64	- `out/run/report.html` / `out/run/report.json` / `out/run/regr_junit.xml` —
   419	    65	  same result in HTML/JSON/JUnit form.
   420	    66	- `out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/` — one directory per test/seed, containing
   421	    67	  `test.bin`, `rtl_sim_stdstreams.log` (see Gotchas), `trace_core_*.log`
   422	    68	  (ibex RTL trace), and `spike_cosim_trace_core_*.log` (Spike reference
   423	    69	  trace).
   424	    70	- `out/build/tb/compile_tb_stdstreams.log` — VCS testbench compile log.
   425	    71	- `out/build/instr_gen/build_stdout.log` — riscv-dv generator VCS build log.
   426	    72	
   427	    73	## Running a regression
   428	    74	
   429	    75	Same `make` invocation, varying `TEST`:
   430	    76	
   431	    77	- `TEST=all` (the Makefile default) — every riscv-dv **and** directed test
   432	    78	  in both testlists.
   433	    79	- `TEST=all_riscvdv` — only tests from `riscv_dv_extension/testlist.yaml`.
   434	    80	- `TEST=all_directed` — only tests from
   435	    81	  `directed_tests/directed_testlist.yaml`.
   436	    82	- `TEST=test_a,test_b` — a comma-separated list of specific test names (mix
   437	    83	  riscv-dv and directed names freely; `all`/`all_riscvdv`/`all_directed` can
   438	    84	  also appear as list entries).
   439	    85	
   440	    86	`ITERATIONS=N` overrides each selected test's iteration count from its
   441	    87	testlist entry. `SEED=S` is the *starting* seed: for a test run with `N`
   442	    88	iterations, seeds `S, S+1, ..., S+N-1` are used, one per iteration
   443	    89	(`out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/` per iteration). If `SEED` is omitted, the
   444	    90	Makefile picks a random starting seed (`$RANDOM`) — pin `SEED` explicitly
   445	    91	for anything you need to reproduce.
   446	    92	
   447	    93	## Coverage
   448	    94	
   449	    95	```bash
   450	    96	source ci/env.sh
   451	    97	cd dv/uvm/core_ibex
   452	    98	make SIMULATOR=vcs IBEX_CONFIG=opentitan ISS=spike \
   453	    99	     TEST=riscv_arithmetic_basic_test ITERATIONS=1 SEED=1 COV=1 OUT=out_cov2
   454	   100	```
   455	   101	
   456	   102	Result: `100.00% PASS 1 PASSED, 0 FAILED`, urg overall score `52.13`
   457	   103	(LINE 70.46, TOGGLE 39.37, FSM 20.93, BRANCH 62.52, ASSERT 85.63,
   458	   104	GROUP 33.86). This run compiles in the FCIBH workaround (see Gotchas) — it
   459	   105	is not a plain vanilla VCS build.
   460	   106	
   461	   107	Artifacts (under `$OUT/run/coverage/`):
   462	   108	
   463	   109	- `merged.vdb` — the urg-merged coverage database.
   464	   110	- `report/` — the urg HTML+text report (`dashboard.txt`/`dashboard.html`,
   465	   111	  `groups.*`, `asserts.*`).
   466	   112	- `fcov/` — riscv-dv's own functional-coverage analysis
   467	   113	  (`test.vdb` + `sim_riscv_instr_cov_test_0_0.log`).
   468	   114	
   469	   115	Full evidence, including the FCIBH root-cause and fix, is in
   470	   116	`docs/dv/evidence/ws1-cov-summary.txt`.
   471	   117	
   472	   118	**Waiver caveat:** `dv/uvm/core_ibex/waivers/aux_code.vRefine` and
   473	   119	`.../unr.vRefine` exist on disk but are **not** referenced by
   474	   120	`scripts/merge_cov.py` or any other script — coverage waivers are not
   475	   121	applied to the urg merge automatically. Load them manually in Verdi/urg if
   476	   122	you need waived coverage numbers.
   477	   123	
   478	   124	## Configs
   479	   125	
   480	   126	`IBEX_CONFIG` selects one of 8 named configs from the top-level
   481	   127	`ibex_configs.yaml`. The DV Makefile's default is `opentitan`
   482	   128	(`dv/uvm/core_ibex/Makefile`); the top-level RTL build Makefile's default is
   483	   129	`small` (`Makefile`) — pass `IBEX_CONFIG` explicitly if you need one
   484	   130	consistently across both.
   485	   131	
   486	   132	| Config | Notes |
   487	   133	|---|---|
   488	   134	| `small` | 2-stage pipeline, no branch-target ALU, 3-cycle mul, no ICache/PMP/SecureIbex. `BaseIsa=RV32I`, `RV32ZC=Zca`. |
   489	   135	| `opentitan` | Matches the OpenTitan project's Ibex integration. `BaseIsa=RV32IorCHERIoT`, ICache+ECC+scramble, SecureIbex, PMP (16 regions), DbgTriggerEn. DV Makefile default. |
   490	   136	| `maxperf` | 3-stage pipeline, branch-target ALU, 1-cycle mul, no ICache/PMP. Max performance ignoring the (unverified) branch predictor. |
   491	   137	| `maxperf-pmp` | `maxperf` + PMP enabled, 16 regions. |
   492	   138	| `maxperf-pmp-bmbalanced` | `maxperf` + PMP + balanced bitmanip (`RV32BBalanced`). |
   493	   139	| `maxperf-pmp-bmfull` | `maxperf-pmp` + full bitmanip (`RV32BFull`). |
   494	   140	| `maxperf-pmp-bmfull-icache` | `maxperf-pmp-bmfull` + ICache/ICacheECC enabled. |
   495	   141	| `experimental-branch-predictor` | `maxperf`-style config with `BranchPredictor=1` — experimental, not fully verified. |
   496	   142	
   497	   143	Each riscv-dv/directed testlist entry may carry an `rtl_params` map (e.g.
   498	   144	`SecureIbex: 1`); `scripts/ibex_cmd.py:filter_tests_by_config()` drops any
   499	   145	test whose `rtl_params` aren't all satisfied by the selected config before
   500	   146	building the regression's test list — e.g. `SecureIbex`-gated tests are
   501	   147	silently excluded from a `small` or `maxperf` run, not failed.
   502	   148	
   503	   149	## Gotchas
   504	   150	
   505	   151	- **Stale `metadata.pickle`.** After editing a testlist or
   506	   152	  `ibex_configs.yaml`, `metadata.py` refuses to recreate metadata if
   507	   153	  `out/metadata/metadata.pickle` already exists from a prior run. Use
   508	   154	  `make clean` or a fresh `OUT=<new_dir>` after such edits.
   509	   155	- **riscv-dv generator is VCS-compiled too.** The instruction generator
   510	   156	  (`out/build/instr_gen/`) is its own separate VCS build, distinct from the
   511	   157	  TB build (`out/build/tb/`) — a fresh regression run consumes two VCS
   512	   158	  builds and two VCS licenses, not one.
   513	   159	- **Site `module` command exits 1 even on success.** This site's Modules
   514	   160	  install prints `Module ERROR: invalid command name "module-hide"` Tcl
   515	   161	  noise on every load (harmless) and returns exit status 1 regardless of
   516	   162	  whether the load succeeded. Never `&&`-chain a `module load`;
   517	   163	  `ci/env.sh` already accounts for this.
   518	   164	- **VCS `-l` quirk: sim stdout lands in `rtl_sim_stdstreams.log`,** not
   519	   165	  `rtl_sim.log` (the latter also exists and duplicates the same content).
   520	   166	  Similarly: TB compile log is
   521	   167	  `out/build/tb/compile_tb_stdstreams.log`; the riscv-dv generator build
   522	   168	  log is `out/build/instr_gen/build_stdout.log`; the per-test
   523	   169	  cross-compile log is `out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/compile.riscvdv.log`
   524	   170	  (0 bytes on a clean compile — benign).
   525	   171	- **`.vRefine` waivers are not applied automatically** — see Coverage
   526	   172	  section above.
   527	   173	- **`WAVES=1` needs `$VERDI_HOME` for FSDB waveforms** (`vcs.tcl`); without
   528	   174	  it, VCS falls back to VPD (`waves.vpd` instead of `waves.fsdb`).
   529	   175	- **FCIBH workaround (VCS-only).** VCS half-implements SystemVerilog's
   530	   176	  `default sequence` for transition-bin coverage: under `COV=1`,
   531	   177	  `core_ibex_fcov_if.sv`'s `cp_controller_fsm`/`cp_controller_fsm_sleep`
   532	   178	  covergroups fatally errored (`Error-[FCIBH] Illegal bin hit`) on legal FSM
   533	   179	  self-loops (e.g. `RESET=>RESET`) because VCS treats every unlisted
   534	   180	  transition as illegal rather than the empty set upstream intended. Fixed
   535	   181	  by guarding the `illegal_bins ... = default sequence;` blocks behind
   536	   182	  `` `ifndef FCOV_NO_DEFAULT_SEQUENCE ``, with that macro defined only in
   537	   183	  the `vcs` tool's compile command in
   538	   184	  `dv/uvm/core_ibex/yaml/rtl_simulation.yaml` (commit `dbba358f`) — no other
   539	   185	  simulator's build is affected. A real fix requires enumerating the FSM's
   540	   186	  legal transitions explicitly instead of relying on `default sequence`;
   541	   187	  out of scope here.
   542	   188	- **`IBEX_CFG_*` define-name mismatch (fixed, commit `54e01775`).** The TB
   543	   189	  (`dv/uvm/core_ibex/tb/core_ibex_tb_top.sv`) previously guarded on
   544	   190	  `IBEX_CFG_BASE_ISA`/`IBEX_CFG_REG_FILE` while `util/ibex_config.py` emits
   545	   191	  `+define+IBEX_CFG_BaseIsa`/`+define+IBEX_CFG_RegFile` — the spelling
   546	   192	  mismatch meant the `` `ifdef ``s never matched, so every config silently
   547	   193	  elaborated with the TB's own CHERIoT-capable defaults regardless of
   548	   194	  `IBEX_CONFIG`. `RV32ZC` had no TB forwarding at all. All three are fixed;
   549	   195	  the TB now prints a `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=... RegFile=... RV32ZC=...`
   550	   196	  banner at time 0 as permanent runtime evidence that a given config
   551	   197	  actually reached elaboration — e.g. `opentitan` prints
   552	   198	  `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=BaseIsaRV32IorCHERIoT RegFile=RegFileFF
   553	   199	  RV32ZC=RV32ZcaZcbZcmp`, `small` prints
   554	   200	  `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=BaseIsaRV32I RegFile=RegFileFF RV32ZC=RV32Zca`
   555	   201	  (see `docs/dv/evidence/ws1-smoke-regr-note.txt`).
   556	   202	- **Direct `scripts/*.py` invocation needs `PYTHONPATH`.** Running any
   557	   203	  `dv/uvm/core_ibex/scripts/*.py` script directly (bypassing `make`) needs
   558	   204	  `PYTHONPATH` set via `scripts.setup_imports.get_pythonpath()` first — the
   559	   205	  Makefile does this automatically for every target; a bare
   560	   206	  `python3 scripts/metadata.py --help` will fail with
   561	   207	  `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'ibex_config'` otherwise.

   576	     1	# Ibex DV principles
   577	     2	
   578	     3	Recurring verification best-practices for this repo — the centralized "how we verify" reference
   579	     4	for humans and agents. It binds hand-written and generated DV equally: tests, TB components,
   580	     5	checkers, coverage, and the tooling around them. Flow commands live in
   581	     6	[`BUILD_AND_SIM.md`](BUILD_AND_SIM.md); cosim architecture in [`COSIM.md`](COSIM.md); the
   582	     7	generated-test interface contract in `TB_CONTRACT.md` (once WS2 lands).
   583	     8	
   584	     9	## 1. Stimulus & drive
   585	    10	
   586	    11	- **Realistic drive at the DUT boundary.** Drive ibex the way a real system does: instructions
   587	    12	  through generated programs (riscv-dv or directed assembly) fetched over the instruction bus,
   588	    13	  data through the memory interface agents, interrupts through the irq lines. A backdoor or
   589	    14	  force is acceptable only where the boundary itself is the thing being modeled (e.g. memory
   590	    15	  model contents) — never as a shortcut around a DUT input you could drive for real. A forced
   591	    16	  internal net can silently no-op under VCS visibility rules; if a backdoor is unavoidable,
   592	    17	  verify it took (write → clock → read back → error on mismatch).
   593	    18	- **If it's a config, program it.** CSR-controlled behavior is set through real CSR-writing
   594	    19	  instructions in the test program, not by poking DUT state. TB-parameter behavior is set
   595	    20	  through the config system (`IBEX_CONFIG`, `+define+IBEX_CFG_*`) — and the `TB-CONFIG:` banner
   596	    21	  exists so every log proves which configuration actually elaborated.
   597	    22	- **Randomize, don't walk.** A test explores its state space to catch corners, not to confirm
   598	    23	  one path. Randomly select stimulus per iteration with distribution weights; pin a directed
   599	    24	  iteration inside the random stream only to guarantee a specific corner. Never reduce a test
   600	    25	  to a single directed case "because the answer is known".
   601	    26	- **Future-proof every count.** Derive counts and ranges from their canonical parameter
   602	    27	  (`ibex_pkg` enums/parameters, config fields), never a re-typed literal — when the design
   603	    28	  parameter changes, the stimulus must follow with no edit.
   604	    29	- **Cover the transition, not just the steady state.** Interleave alternative modes (e.g.
   605	    30	  interrupt on/off phases, debug entries, PMP region reconfiguration) so the switch itself is
   606	    31	  exercised, not just each mode in isolation.
   607	    32	- **Own your preconditions.** A test programs the machine state its behavior depends on
   608	    33	  (privilege mode, MIE/MSTATUS, PMP setup) explicitly at entry and assumes nothing about
   609	    34	  incoming state; on observing unexpected state, error rather than adapt silently.
   610	    35	- **Sensitizing operands.** Distinct, non-degenerate data; interpretation-distinct values so a
   611	    36	  wrong interpretation is observable. No all-zeros or identity values that mask a fault.
   612	    37	
   613	    38	## 2. Checking & correctness
   614	    39	
   615	    40	- **Check at the interface boundary; internal signals only when unavoidable.** Prefer checks at
   616	    41	  module/bus boundaries and architectural state (the cosim comparison is the model: retired
   617	    42	  instructions, RF writes, PC). An internal-signal check is fragile to design change and does
   618	    43	  not show end-to-end behavior — when one is unavoidable, make its failure message identify
   619	    44	  itself ("checked signal moved/renamed", not a phantom bug) and pair it with an end-to-end
   620	    45	  check.
   621	    46	- **Deterministic ⇒ derive from intent.** A reference model computes expected values from the
   622	    47	  ISA/spec and the driven stimulus — never by copying an RTL decode net. A model mirrored from
   623	    48	  the RTL only proves the RTL matches itself. Reach for an internal anchor only when the
   624	    49	  quantity's starting state is genuinely unknowable from intent, and document it.
   625	    50	- **End-to-end round-trip.** Use data again through the consumer's output (store then load
   626	    51	  back; CSR write then read). Handing data off correctly does not prove it was applied.
   627	    52	- **Place the check where the failure lands — never before it.** A check that passes before
   628	    53	  full propagation is a false pass waiting to happen; gate on the commit/settle event.
   629	    54	- **Verify the result of every transaction — including the "harmless" ones.** A read issued
   630	    55	  only to observe state must still assert both that it landed and that the value matches; a
   631	    56	  dropped or perturbing read otherwise passes unnoticed.
   632	    57	- **Fail through a mechanism the flow actually collects.** In the SV-UVM flow, that is
   633	    58	  `uvm_error`/`uvm_fatal` (scanned by `scripts/check_logs.py` into `trr.yaml`/`regr.log`); in
   634	    59	  cocotb tests, a Python `assert`/raised exception fails the cocotb test which fails the run. A
   635	    60	  bare `$display`/log line fails nothing. Know your failure path and prove it once (see §6).
   636	    61	- **Predict-and-check.** Per-feature expectations computed from randomized stimulus + sampled
   637	    62	  config, compared against observation. Keep predictors pure and host-testable where possible.
   638	    63	- **Ground-truth at the waveform for new checkers.** Confirm a new monitor/checker against the
   639	    64	  actual wave at the cycles of interest at least once — it catches mis-reads (wrong hierarchical
   640	    65	  handle, packed-signal misinterpretation, silent-idle None handle) that a green test hides.
   641	    66	
   642	    67	## 3. Scoreboard & infrastructure
   643	    68	
   644	    69	- **Passive, always-on checking is the default.** The best check is one a test author cannot
   645	    70	  forget: derived automatically, applied on every transaction in every test (the cosim referee
   646	    71	  is the archetype). Active in-test checks exist for (a) "did the targeted scenario actually
   647	    72	  fire" — the reason the test exists — and (b) checks unique or too costly to run passively.
   648	    73	  Both layers are needed; a passive floor does not remove a test's duty to check its own
   649	    74	  driven data and effects.
   650	    75	- **Always-true invariants live in shared infrastructure; "did it fire" lives in the test.**
   651	    76	- **No-modify reuse of shared/vendored infra.** riscv-dv and lowrisc_ip are vendored: extend
   652	    77	  through the extension points (`riscv_dv_extension/`, TB hooks), don't patch vendored code
   653	    78	  ad-hoc. When a genuine vendor patch is required it goes through `vendor/patches/` with the
   654	    79	  vendoring flow, never a direct edit.
   655	    80	
   656	    81	## 4. RTL truth & honesty
   657	    82	
   658	    83	- **Ground in intent, log divergence.** Models and testplans derive from the ISA spec and
   659	    84	  documented intent. When RTL and intent disagree: if the RTL is more complete/correct, RTL
   660	    85	  wins and the disagreement is logged; if the RTL is less complete than the documented intent,
   661	    86	  that is a latent-bug candidate — write the test to the intended behavior, mark it as a known
   662	    87	  expected-fail, and drive the fix. DV does not modify RTL to make tests pass.
   663	    88	- **Don't hide failures.** Never fake-pass, silently down-scope, or paper over something that
   664	    89	  cannot be driven or checked; surface limitations explicitly (the FCIBH workaround in
   665	    90	  `BUILD_AND_SIM.md` is the pattern: documented, scoped, with the follow-up named).
   666	    91	- **Evidence over inference.** If a question is answerable from waves, RTL, docs, git history,
   667	    92	  or a quick experiment — check it. State unverified claims as unverified. A mechanism you
   668	    93	  explained but did not observe is a guess.
   669	    94	- **FCOV: build samplable, prune impossible.** A coverpoint may be built before stimulus can
   670	    95	  hit it (0% marks intent), but genuinely unhittable bins are pruned/`ignore_bins`'d so
   671	    96	  coverage isn't diluted. No duplicate coverpoints — extend, don't re-cover. Beware simulator
   672	    97	  semantics: `illegal_bins = default sequence` is not portable (see the FCIBH gotcha).
   673	    98	
   674	    99	## 5. Tooling & hygiene
   675	   100	
   676	   101	- **Toggleable debug, never unconditional prints.** Debug output gates behind a plusarg;
   677	   102	  plusarg names are declared once (test/TB constants), not scattered string literals — a
   678	   103	  mistyped gate silently no-ops.
   679	   104	- **Concise diagnostics.** A failure message states its triggering condition — id plus
   680	   105	  expected-vs-actual — nothing more. Root-cause narration belongs in docs, not runtime messages.
   681	   106	- **Self-sufficient docs and code.** Every doc, commit message, comment, and identifier is
   682	   107	  resolvable on its own: expand a tag/ID on first use or link the defining doc. Code never
   683	   108	  depends on a bare cross-file positional index or opaque tag; bind by named import or stable
   684	   109	  key that fails loud on reorder.
   685	   110	- **Concise code comments — intent only.** What and why, never history ("moved from X",
   686	   111	  "previously Y") — that lives in git.
   687	   112	- **Single source of truth.** A value meaning the same thing in two places is defined once and
   688	   113	  imported: `ibex_pkg` parameters, config fields, one constants home per language domain.
   689	   114	- **No hardcoded paths — filesystem or hierarchy.** Filesystem paths anchor to the repo root
   690	   115	  or `ci/env.sh`-exported variables. Hierarchical RTL references (SV bind paths, cocotb
   691	   116	  handles) resolve through one central place per domain (the fcov binds; the cocotb common
   692	   117	  package), so a rename fails loudly there instead of silently per call-site.
   693	   118	- **Site realities** (see `BUILD_AND_SIM.md` gotchas): the module command exits nonzero on
   694	   119	  success; background notifications are unreliable — poll artifacts with deadlines and watchdog
   695	   120	  long-running work; clear `PYTHONPATH` around pip operations.
   696	   121	
   697	   122	## 6. Trust & evidence
   698	   123	
   699	   124	Every claim about verification is backed by committed evidence — logs, reports, banners — not
   700	   125	prose. The `TB-CONFIG:` banner, the evidence files under `docs/dv/evidence/`, and the review
   701	   126	artifacts under `docs/dv/reviews/` are the standing examples.
   702	   127	
   703	   128	<!-- TRUST-TRIAD-CANONICAL-BEGIN -->
   704	   129	**The trust triad** — required for every new test, checker, assertion, or covergroup, whether
   705	   130	human-written or generated:
   706	   131	
   707	   132	1. **TDD** — the behavior is specified by a failing check before the implementation that makes
   708	   133	   it pass; the red→green transcript is part of the evidence.
   709	   134	2. **Mutation-proof** — a new checker or assertion counts only when a named mutation (recorded
   710	   135	   as id, file:line, original, mutated, expected detector) is caught by the NAMED checker with
   711	   136	   hidden referees inert, plus a checker-ablation negative control (checker disabled ⇒ the
   712	   137	   mutation survives). "Hidden referees inert" operationally: run with `+disable_cosim=1` (cosim
   713	   138	   mismatches become informational and cannot fail the test) and verify the failure signature in
   714	   139	   the log belongs to the named checker.
   715	   140	3. **fcov-expectation** — every new test declares the functional-coverage bins it intends to
   716	   141	   hit; declared-but-unhit bins FAIL the run. Verification is per-test and pre-merge (merged
   717	   142	   databases let one test claim another's bins), generated covergroups live in an isolated
   718	   143	   namespace, and every sampling condition gets an anti-vacuity review (a bin hit by an
   719	   144	   always-true sample proves nothing).
   720	   145	<!-- TRUST-TRIAD-CANONICAL-END -->
   721	   146	
   722	   147	- **Self-proving checks.** The triad's rules 2 and 3 generalize: a check never exercised to
   723	   148	  fail is not trusted; a stimulus never proven to arrive is not trusted.
   724	   149	- **Reviewer independence.** The executing model never self-approves — the cross-model review
   725	   150	  policy in `CLAUDE.md` governs; artifacts live under `docs/dv/reviews/`.
   726	   151	- **No hidden-referee reliance.** Generated DV must carry its own checking and may not lean on
   727	   152	  referees it cannot see (the cosim demotion rules and the checking obligation live in the
   728	   153	  spec's fence workstream and `TB_CONTRACT.md` once WS2 lands).
   729

  1020	    25	--------------------------------------------------------------------
  1021	    26	Fix history: FCIBH illegal-bin-hit workaround
  1022	    27	--------------------------------------------------------------------
  1023	    28	The first COV=1 attempt (OUT=out_cov) FAILED the test: VCS raised a
  1024	    29	fatal coverage "VERIFICATION ERROR" and aborted simulation after only
  1025	    30	2064350 ps:
  1026	    31	
  1027	    32	  Error-[FCIBH] Illegal bin hit
  1028	    33	  dv/uvm/core_ibex/fcov/core_ibex_fcov_if.sv, 588 (covergroup core_ibex_fcov_if::uarch_cg)
  1029	    34	  Illegal transition bin illegal_transitions of coverpoint cp_controller_fsm
  1030	    35	  got hit with transition RESET=>RESET
  1031	    36	
  1032	    37	Cause: `illegal_bins illegal_transitions = default sequence;` on
  1033	    38	cp_controller_fsm / cp_controller_fsm_sleep relies on SystemVerilog's
  1034	    39	`default sequence`, which VCS does not implement (the source already
  1035	    40	carried an in-source TODO to this effect since 2022, commit aee235cfa).
  1036	    41	VCS instead treats every unlisted transition — including legal
  1037	    42	self-loops like RESET=>RESET — as an illegal-bin hit, which is fatal.
  1038	    43	This only surfaces once the fcov covergroups are built/sampled, i.e.
  1039	    44	only under COV=1 (Task 6's COV=0 run never enabled +enable_ibex_fcov or
  1040	    45	-cm and so never hit it).
  1041	    46	
  1042	    47	Fix (VCS-only, gated by a compile-time macro so no other simulator's
  1043	    48	build is affected):
  1044	    49	
  1045	    50	  dv/uvm/core_ibex/fcov/core_ibex_fcov_if.sv
  1046	    51	    Both `illegal_bins illegal_transitions = default sequence;` blocks
  1047	    52	    (cp_controller_fsm, cp_controller_fsm_sleep) wrapped in
  1048	    53	    `` `ifndef FCOV_NO_DEFAULT_SEQUENCE `` / `` `endif ``.
  1049	    54	
  1050	    55	  dv/uvm/core_ibex/yaml/rtl_simulation.yaml
  1051	    56	    `+define+FCOV_NO_DEFAULT_SEQUENCE` added to the vcs tool's compile
  1052	    57	    cmd only (no other simulator entry touched).
  1053	    58	
  1054	    59	Confirmed clean of FCIBH hits on the final rerun too
  1055	    60	(`grep -c "FCIBH" out_ws1_cov/run/tests/riscv_arithmetic_basic_test.1/rtl_sim_stdstreams.log`
  1056	    61	== 0, see ws1-manifest.txt for the run this was checked against).

  1200	    57	
  1201	    58	## The Rule
  1202	    59	
  1203	    60	**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action** — including clarifying questions, exploring the codebase, or checking files. If it turns out wrong for the situation, you don't have to use it.
  1204	    61	
  1205	    62	**Before entering plan mode:** if you haven't already brainstormed, invoke the brainstorming skill first.
  1206	    63	
  1207	    64	Then announce "Using [skill] to [purpose]" and follow the skill exactly. If it has a checklist, create a todo per item.
  1208	    65	
  1209	    66	## Skill Priority
  1210	    67	
  1211	    68	When multiple skills apply, process skills come first — they set the approach, then implementation skills (frontend-design, etc.) carry it out. Brainstorming and systematic-debugging are Superpowers' most common process skills, but the rule holds for any of them.
  1212	    69	
  1213	    70	- "Let's build X" → superpowers:brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
  1214	    71	- "Fix this bug" → superpowers:systematic-debugging first, then domain skills.
  1215	    72	
  1216	    73	## Red Flags
  1217	    74	
  1218	    75	These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:
  1219	    76	
  1220	    77	| Thought | Reality |
  1221	    78	|---------|---------|
  1222	    79	| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
  1223	    80	| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
  1224	    81	| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
  1225	    82	| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
  1226	    83	| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
  1227	    84	| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
  1228	    85	| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
  1229	    86	| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
  1230	    87	| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
  1231	    88	| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
  1232	    89	| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
  1233	    90	| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ≠ using the skill. Invoke it. |

  1260	   117	# Ibex auto-DV fork — canonical agent instructions
  1261	   118	
  1262	   119	This file is the single source of truth for AI agents in this repo. `AGENTS.md` (codex) delegates
  1263	   120	here and mirrors only the Critical Invariants block below.
  1264	   121	
  1265	   122	## Environment & flow
  1266	   123	
  1267	   124	- `source ci/env.sh` first, always (login shell / `bash -lc`; see its header for the supported-shell
  1268	   125	  contract). One-time setup and every verified build/run/coverage command: `docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md`.
  1269	   126	- The UVM DV flow lives in `dv/uvm/core_ibex` (Makefile-driven; VCS via `SIMULATOR=vcs`); cosim
  1270	   127	  architecture: `docs/dv/COSIM.md`; generated-test interface: `docs/dv/TB_CONTRACT.md` (once WS2 lands).
  1271	   128	
  1272	   129	## DV principles
  1273	   130	
  1274	   131	`docs/dv/dv_principles.md` binds all DV work, hand-written or generated. In one line: realistic
  1275	   132	boundary stimulus, intent-derived checking that fails through a collected mechanism, honesty over
  1276	   133	green, and the trust triad — TDD, mutation-proof, fcov-expectation — for every new test or checker.
  1277	   134	
  1278	   135	## Cross-model review policy
  1279	   136	
  1280	   137	- The executing model never self-approves.
  1281	   138	- Before execution: the plan/spec/testplan is reviewed by the other model (Claude executes ⇒ codex
  1282	   139	  reviews; codex executes ⇒ `claude -p` reviews). After execution: the diff is reviewed by the other
  1283	   140	  model the same way.
  1284	   141	- Review artifacts are committed under `docs/dv/reviews/`, each with a reviewer-identity header (CLI
  1285	   142	  version, model ID, reasoning setting) and an explicit review target (commit SHAs / diff scope).
  1286	   143	- Verdicts are machine-readable — `APPROVE` / `APPROVE-WITH-CHANGES` / `REQUEST-CHANGES` — and
  1287	   144	  gating: no progress past `REQUEST-CHANGES` without a recorded re-review.
  1288	   145	- Disagreements with a recorded controller ruling go to the human owner, not back into the loop.
  1289	   146	- The `cross-review` skill executes this policy; use it rather than ad-hoc invocations.
  1290	   147	
  1291	   148	## Critical invariants
  1292	   149	
  1293	   150	<!-- CRITICAL-INVARIANTS-BEGIN -->
  1294	   151	1. The executing model never self-approves: plans get a pre-execution review and diffs get a
  1295	   152	   post-execution review by the other model; a `REQUEST-CHANGES` verdict blocks progress until a
  1296	   153	   recorded re-review reaches `APPROVE`/`APPROVE-WITH-CHANGES`.
  1297	   154	2. Knowledge-fence: generation sessions must not read fenced DV collateral. Fence rules live in
  1298	   155	   `docs/dv/FENCE.md` once WS7 lands; until a fence authority exists, generation sessions are
  1299	   156	   NOT permitted at all (fail closed), and infra sessions never paste fenced content into
  1300	   157	   allowed files.
  1301	   158	<!-- CRITICAL-INVARIANTS-END -->
  1302	   159	
  1303	   160	## Site gotchas
  1304	   161	
  1305	   162	- The `module` command exits 1 even on success — never `&&`-chain it (`ci/env.sh` handles loads).
  1306	   163	- Background/completion notifications are unreliable: poll log files and artifacts directly with
  1307	   164	  deadlines, and put a watchdog timer on any dispatched work longer than ~5 minutes.
  1308	   165	- Clear `PYTHONPATH` around pip operations (`~/.bashrc` leaks package metadata into `pip freeze`).
  1309	   166	- Stale `metadata.pickle`: use `make clean` or a fresh `OUT=` after editing testlists/configs.
  1310	   167	- Full gotcha list with evidence: `docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md`.
  1311	   168

  1316	   173	`lint-check`, `dv-principles-check`, `mutation-check`, `fcov-expectation`, `simple-english`.

  1376	   233	## Subagent dispatch requires multi-agent support
  1377	   234	
  1378	   235	Add to your Codex config (`~/.codex/config.toml`):
  1379	   236	
  1380	   237	```toml
  1381	   238	[features]
  1382	   239	multi_agent = true
  1383	   240	```
  1384	   241	
  1385	   242	This enables the multi-agent tools that skills like
  1386	   243	`dispatching-parallel-agents` and `subagent-driven-development` use.
  1387	   244	Which tools you get depends on the multi-agent version your model
  1388	   245	preset selects (current presets run V2; older ones run V1). Trust your
  1389	   246	actual tool list over any table — including this one — when they
  1390	   247	disagree.
  1391	   248	
  1392	   249	- **Spawning:** give children a clean context with
  1393	   250	  `spawn_agent {fork_turns: "none"}`; the default `"all"` copies your
  1394	   251	  entire transcript into the child. On Codex 0.145+, role files under
  1395	   252	  `~/.codex/agents/` attach to isolated forks via `agent_type`.
  1396	   253	  Full-history forks accept `model` and `reasoning_effort` overrides
  1397	   254	  (only `agent_type` is refused there) — isolated forks are the SDD
  1398	   255	  default for context hygiene, not because overrides require them.
  1399	   256	- **Fix rounds:** resume the implementer with `followup_task` — it
  1400	   257	  delivers your message, triggers a turn, and transparently reloads a
  1401	   258	  child the harness evicted. Never dispatch a fresh implementer on the
  1402	   259	  theory that a spawned agent cannot be messaged again; on V2 it
  1403	   260	  always can.
  1404	   261	- **Lifecycle:** V2 has no `close_agent`. Finished children are
  1405	   262	  evicted automatically when slots are needed; leaving them unclosed
  1406	   263	  costs nothing. Only V1 sessions have `close_agent` — there, close
  1407	   264	  reviewers when their review returns, and close each implementer
  1408	   265	  after its task's review passes.
  1409	   266	- **Model names:** never copy a model name from a skill, table, or old
  1410	   267	  session into `spawn_agent` without checking it against your current
  1411	   268	  spawn allowlist — V2 accepts only V2-capable presets and hard-errors
  1412	   269	  on the rest.
  1413	   270	
  1414	   271	## Waiting on children
  1415	   272	
  1416	   273	`wait_agent` is an event subscription, not a poll: a long wait wakes
  1417	   274	the moment a child produces mailbox activity, with the same latency as
  1418	   275	a short one. Short-timeout polling buys nothing and costs a tool call —
  1419	   276	and a context rebill — per poll. In measured sessions, roughly
  1420	   277	two-thirds of all wait calls were short polls that timed out.
  1421	   278	
  1422	   279	- While you still have local work, do not wait at all. A completed
  1423	   280	  child's final answer is pushed into your mailbox and arrives with
  1424	   281	  your next turn.
  1425	   282	- When you are genuinely idle with children outstanding, wait in
  1426	   283	  bounded stretches: `wait_agent` with `timeout_ms` 300000-600000
  1427	   284	  (5-10 minutes). After each stretch — wake or timeout — post one
  1428	   285	  status line, run `list_agents`, and chase any child that finished
  1429	   286	  without reporting. Never stack polls shorter than five minutes; the
  1430	   287	  event subscription wakes a bounded stretch just as fast as a short
  1431	   288	  one.
  1432	   289	- Completion mail cannot wake an idle controller (it is delivered
  1433	   290	  without triggering a turn); covering that idle window is
  1434	   291	  `wait_agent`'s only job. A stretch that times out with no activity
  1435	   292	  is your cue to reconcile, not to shorten the next stretch.
  1436	   293	
  1437	   294	## Model routing on spawns
  1438	   295	
  1439	   296	Every `spawn_agent` you issue — including when you are yourself a
  1440	   297	spawned child running a fan-out — sets `model` AND `reasoning_effort`
  1441	   298	explicitly, per the Model Selection rules of the skill you are
  1442	   299	executing. Setting `model` alone is a trap: the child's effort
  1443	   300	silently resets to that model's default, not to yours.
  1444	   301	
  1445	   302	Ask your human partner to add a machine-level backstop to
  1446	   303	`~/.codex/config.toml` so any spawn that slips through still routes to
  1447	   304	a deliberate tier instead of silently inheriting the session's most
  1448	   305	expensive model:
  1449	   306	
  1450	   307	```toml
  1451	   308	[agents]
  1452	   309	default_subagent_model = "<a mid-tier model from your spawn allowlist>"
  1453	   310	default_subagent_reasoning_effort = "medium"
  1454	   311	```
  1455	   312	
  1456	   313	## Environment Detection
  1457	   314	
  1458	   315	Skills that create worktrees or finish branches should detect their
  1459	   316	environment with read-only git commands before proceeding:
  1460	   317	
  1461	   318	```bash
  1462	   319	GIT_DIR=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
  1463	   320	GIT_COMMON=$(cd "$(git rev-parse --git-common-dir)" 2>/dev/null && pwd -P)
  1464	   321	BRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)
  1465	   322	```
  1466	   323	
  1467	   324	- `GIT_DIR != GIT_COMMON` → already in a linked worktree (skip creation)
  1468	   325	- `BRANCH` empty → detached HEAD (cannot branch/push/PR from sandbox)
  1469	   326	
  1470	   327	See `using-git-worktrees` Step 0 and `finishing-a-development-branch`
  1471	   328	Step 1 for how each skill uses these signals.
  1472	   329	
  1473	   330	## Codex App Finishing
  1474	   331	
  1475	   332	When the sandbox blocks branch/push operations (detached HEAD in an
  1476	   333	externally managed worktree), the agent commits all work and informs
  1477	   334	the user to use the App's native controls:
  1478	   335	
  1479	   336	- **"Create branch"** — names the branch, then commit/push/PR via App UI
  1480	   337	- **"Hand off to local"** — transfers work to the user's local checkout
  1481	   338	
  1482	   339	The agent can still run tests, stage files, and output suggested branch
  1483	   340	names, commit messages, and PR descriptions for the user to copy.

  1498	   355	     1	# Building and Running Ibex DV Simulations (VCS)
  1499	   356	     2	
  1500	   357	     3	This document covers the `core_ibex` UVM DV flow (`dv/uvm/core_ibex`) under
  1501	   358	     4	Synopsys VCS with Spike as the co-simulator, on this site's tooling. Every
  1502	   359	     5	command below was actually run during WS1 bring-up; wall-times are what was
  1503	   360	     6	observed on this host (a 192-core machine), not vendor/upstream estimates.
  1504	   361	     7	
  1505	   362	     8	## One-time setup
  1506	   363	     9	
  1507	   364	    10	Run once per checkout (or whenever the tools move):
  1508	   365	    11	
  1509	   366	    12	If you are not fzhang, first `export IBEX_TOOLS_DIR=/path/to/writable/dir` —
  1510	   367	    13	it defaults to `/localdev/fzhang/ws/tools`.
  1511	   368	    14	
  1512	   369	    15	```bash
  1513	   370	    16	source ci/env.sh
  1514	   371	    17	bash ci/setup-venv.sh
  1515	   372	    18	bash ci/build-spike.sh
  1516	   373	    19	bash ci/get-toolchain.sh
  1517	   374	    20	```
  1518	   375	    21	
  1519	   376	    22	- `ci/env.sh` is the single source of truth for tool paths: it loads the
  1520	   377	    23	  site's VCS/licenses/dtc Modules (see Gotchas below), and exports
  1521	   378	    24	  `IBEX_PYTHON`, `RISCV_GCC`/`RISCV_TOOLCHAIN`, `SPIKE_PATH`, and the venv's
  1522	   379	    25	  `PKG_CONFIG_PATH`. Source it in every new shell before running anything
  1523	   380	    26	  else in this document.
  1524	   381	    27	- `ci/setup-venv.sh` creates `.venv/` and installs from `ci/requirements.lock`
  1525	   382	    28	  when present, falling back to `python-requirements.txt` (including
  1526	   383	    29	  riscv-dv's own requirements) otherwise, via `$IBEX_PYTHON`. Idempotent —
  1527	   384	    30	  safe to re-run.
  1528	   385	    31	- `ci/build-spike.sh` clones and builds the lowRISC fork of Spike (pinned
  1529	   386	    32	  revision) and installs it to `$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/spike-ibex-cosim`. Requires
  1530	   387	    33	  `dtc` on PATH, which `ci/env.sh` loads via Modules. Observed: ~51s wall
  1531	   388	    34	  time on this host.
  1532	   389	    35	- `ci/get-toolchain.sh` downloads and unpacks the lowRISC
  1533	   390	    36	  `rv32imcb` prebuilt GCC toolchain to
  1534	   391	    37	  `$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/lowrisc-toolchain-gcc-rv32imcb`. Observed: ~1s to
  1535	   392	    38	  download the 41.8M tarball on this host's network; idempotent (skips the
  1536	   393	    39	  download if the tarball is already present).
  1537	   394	    40	
  1538	   395	    41	## Running a single test
  1539	   396	    42	
  1540	   397	    43	```bash
  1541	   398	    44	source ci/env.sh
  1542	   399	    45	cd dv/uvm/core_ibex
  1543	   400	    46	make clean
  1544	   401	    47	make SIMULATOR=vcs IBEX_CONFIG=opentitan ISS=spike \
  1545	   402	    48	     TEST=riscv_arithmetic_basic_test ITERATIONS=1 SEED=1
  1546	   403	    49	```
  1547	   404	    50	
  1548	   405	    51	Observed: **~72s total wall time** on this host for a fully fresh build
  1549	   406	    52	(TB compile ~39s of that; the flow's own once-off estimates for this class
  1550	   407	    53	of build are much larger — 30-60 min — but do not hold on this host). A
  1551	   408	    54	second from-scratch run during doc verification took ~156s instead (same
  1552	   409	    55	~39s TB compile, extra time in env/module-load overhead) — expect some
  1553	   410	    56	run-to-run variance on this host, not just this one number.
  1554	   411	    57	
  1555	   412	    58	This one `make` invocation runs the whole flow: riscv-dv instruction
  1556	   413	    59	generation, cross-compile to a binary, VCS testbench build, and the VCS +
  1557	   414	    60	Spike cosim run. Results land under `out/`:
  1558	   415	    61	
  1559	   416	    62	- `out/run/regr.log` — pass/fail summary (`100.00% PASS 1 PASSED, 0 FAILED`
  1560	   417	    63	  on success).
  1561	   418	    64	- `out/run/report.html` / `out/run/report.json` / `out/run/regr_junit.xml` —
  1562	   419	    65	  same result in HTML/JSON/JUnit form.
  1563	   420	    66	- `out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/` — one directory per test/seed, containing
  1564	   421	    67	  `test.bin`, `rtl_sim_stdstreams.log` (see Gotchas), `trace_core_*.log`
  1565	   422	    68	  (ibex RTL trace), and `spike_cosim_trace_core_*.log` (Spike reference
  1566	   423	    69	  trace).
  1567	   424	    70	- `out/build/tb/compile_tb_stdstreams.log` — VCS testbench compile log.
  1568	   425	    71	- `out/build/instr_gen/build_stdout.log` — riscv-dv generator VCS build log.
  1569	   426	    72	
  1570	   427	    73	## Running a regression
  1571	   428	    74	
  1572	   429	    75	Same `make` invocation, varying `TEST`:
  1573	   430	    76	
  1574	   431	    77	- `TEST=all` (the Makefile default) — every riscv-dv **and** directed test
  1575	   432	    78	  in both testlists.
  1576	   433	    79	- `TEST=all_riscvdv` — only tests from `riscv_dv_extension/testlist.yaml`.
  1577	   434	    80	- `TEST=all_directed` — only tests from
  1578	   435	    81	  `directed_tests/directed_testlist.yaml`.
  1579	   436	    82	- `TEST=test_a,test_b` — a comma-separated list of specific test names (mix
  1580	   437	    83	  riscv-dv and directed names freely; `all`/`all_riscvdv`/`all_directed` can
  1581	   438	    84	  also appear as list entries).
  1582	   439	    85	
  1583	   440	    86	`ITERATIONS=N` overrides each selected test's iteration count from its
  1584	   441	    87	testlist entry. `SEED=S` is the *starting* seed: for a test run with `N`
  1585	   442	    88	iterations, seeds `S, S+1, ..., S+N-1` are used, one per iteration
  1586	   443	    89	(`out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/` per iteration). If `SEED` is omitted, the
  1587	   444	    90	Makefile picks a random starting seed (`$RANDOM`) — pin `SEED` explicitly
  1588	   445	    91	for anything you need to reproduce.
  1589	   446	    92	
  1590	   447	    93	## Coverage
  1591	   448	    94	
  1592	   449	    95	```bash
  1593	   450	    96	source ci/env.sh
  1594	   451	    97	cd dv/uvm/core_ibex
  1595	   452	    98	make SIMULATOR=vcs IBEX_CONFIG=opentitan ISS=spike \
  1596	   453	    99	     TEST=riscv_arithmetic_basic_test ITERATIONS=1 SEED=1 COV=1 OUT=out_cov2
  1597	   454	   100	```
  1598	   455	   101	
  1599	   456	   102	Result: `100.00% PASS 1 PASSED, 0 FAILED`, urg overall score `52.13`
  1600	   457	   103	(LINE 70.46, TOGGLE 39.37, FSM 20.93, BRANCH 62.52, ASSERT 85.63,
  1601	   458	   104	GROUP 33.86). This run compiles in the FCIBH workaround (see Gotchas) — it
  1602	   459	   105	is not a plain vanilla VCS build.
  1603	   460	   106	
  1604	   461	   107	Artifacts (under `$OUT/run/coverage/`):
  1605	   462	   108	
  1606	   463	   109	- `merged.vdb` — the urg-merged coverage database.
  1607	   464	   110	- `report/` — the urg HTML+text report (`dashboard.txt`/`dashboard.html`,
  1608	   465	   111	  `groups.*`, `asserts.*`).
  1609	   466	   112	- `fcov/` — riscv-dv's own functional-coverage analysis
  1610	   467	   113	  (`test.vdb` + `sim_riscv_instr_cov_test_0_0.log`).
  1611	   468	   114	
  1612	   469	   115	Full evidence, including the FCIBH root-cause and fix, is in
  1613	   470	   116	`docs/dv/evidence/ws1-cov-summary.txt`.
  1614	   471	   117	
  1615	   472	   118	**Waiver caveat:** `dv/uvm/core_ibex/waivers/aux_code.vRefine` and
  1616	   473	   119	`.../unr.vRefine` exist on disk but are **not** referenced by
  1617	   474	   120	`scripts/merge_cov.py` or any other script — coverage waivers are not
  1618	   475	   121	applied to the urg merge automatically. Load them manually in Verdi/urg if
  1619	   476	   122	you need waived coverage numbers.
  1620	   477	   123	
  1621	   478	   124	## Configs
  1622	   479	   125	
  1623	   480	   126	`IBEX_CONFIG` selects one of 8 named configs from the top-level
  1624	   481	   127	`ibex_configs.yaml`. The DV Makefile's default is `opentitan`
  1625	   482	   128	(`dv/uvm/core_ibex/Makefile`); the top-level RTL build Makefile's default is
  1626	   483	   129	`small` (`Makefile`) — pass `IBEX_CONFIG` explicitly if you need one
  1627	   484	   130	consistently across both.
  1628	   485	   131	
  1629	   486	   132	| Config | Notes |
  1630	   487	   133	|---|---|
  1631	   488	   134	| `small` | 2-stage pipeline, no branch-target ALU, 3-cycle mul, no ICache/PMP/SecureIbex. `BaseIsa=RV32I`, `RV32ZC=Zca`. |
  1632	   489	   135	| `opentitan` | Matches the OpenTitan project's Ibex integration. `BaseIsa=RV32IorCHERIoT`, ICache+ECC+scramble, SecureIbex, PMP (16 regions), DbgTriggerEn. DV Makefile default. |
  1633	   490	   136	| `maxperf` | 3-stage pipeline, branch-target ALU, 1-cycle mul, no ICache/PMP. Max performance ignoring the (unverified) branch predictor. |
  1634	   491	   137	| `maxperf-pmp` | `maxperf` + PMP enabled, 16 regions. |
  1635	   492	   138	| `maxperf-pmp-bmbalanced` | `maxperf` + PMP + balanced bitmanip (`RV32BBalanced`). |
  1636	   493	   139	| `maxperf-pmp-bmfull` | `maxperf-pmp` + full bitmanip (`RV32BFull`). |
  1637	   494	   140	| `maxperf-pmp-bmfull-icache` | `maxperf-pmp-bmfull` + ICache/ICacheECC enabled. |
  1638	   495	   141	| `experimental-branch-predictor` | `maxperf`-style config with `BranchPredictor=1` — experimental, not fully verified. |
  1639	   496	   142	
  1640	   497	   143	Each riscv-dv/directed testlist entry may carry an `rtl_params` map (e.g.
  1641	   498	   144	`SecureIbex: 1`); `scripts/ibex_cmd.py:filter_tests_by_config()` drops any
  1642	   499	   145	test whose `rtl_params` aren't all satisfied by the selected config before
  1643	   500	   146	building the regression's test list — e.g. `SecureIbex`-gated tests are
  1644	   501	   147	silently excluded from a `small` or `maxperf` run, not failed.
  1645	   502	   148	
  1646	   503	   149	## Gotchas
  1647	   504	   150	
  1648	   505	   151	- **Stale `metadata.pickle`.** After editing a testlist or
  1649	   506	   152	  `ibex_configs.yaml`, `metadata.py` refuses to recreate metadata if
  1650	   507	   153	  `out/metadata/metadata.pickle` already exists from a prior run. Use
  1651	   508	   154	  `make clean` or a fresh `OUT=<new_dir>` after such edits.
  1652	   509	   155	- **riscv-dv generator is VCS-compiled too.** The instruction generator
  1653	   510	   156	  (`out/build/instr_gen/`) is its own separate VCS build, distinct from the
  1654	   511	   157	  TB build (`out/build/tb/`) — a fresh regression run consumes two VCS
  1655	   512	   158	  builds and two VCS licenses, not one.
  1656	   513	   159	- **Site `module` command exits 1 even on success.** This site's Modules
  1657	   514	   160	  install prints `Module ERROR: invalid command name "module-hide"` Tcl
  1658	   515	   161	  noise on every load (harmless) and returns exit status 1 regardless of
  1659	   516	   162	  whether the load succeeded. Never `&&`-chain a `module load`;
  1660	   517	   163	  `ci/env.sh` already accounts for this.
  1661	   518	   164	- **VCS `-l` quirk: sim stdout lands in `rtl_sim_stdstreams.log`,** not
  1662	   519	   165	  `rtl_sim.log` (the latter also exists and duplicates the same content).
  1663	   520	   166	  Similarly: TB compile log is
  1664	   521	   167	  `out/build/tb/compile_tb_stdstreams.log`; the riscv-dv generator build
  1665	   522	   168	  log is `out/build/instr_gen/build_stdout.log`; the per-test
  1666	   523	   169	  cross-compile log is `out/run/tests/<test>.<seed>/compile.riscvdv.log`
  1667	   524	   170	  (0 bytes on a clean compile — benign).
  1668	   525	   171	- **`.vRefine` waivers are not applied automatically** — see Coverage
  1669	   526	   172	  section above.
  1670	   527	   173	- **`WAVES=1` needs `$VERDI_HOME` for FSDB waveforms** (`vcs.tcl`); without
  1671	   528	   174	  it, VCS falls back to VPD (`waves.vpd` instead of `waves.fsdb`).
  1672	   529	   175	- **FCIBH workaround (VCS-only).** VCS half-implements SystemVerilog's
  1673	   530	   176	  `default sequence` for transition-bin coverage: under `COV=1`,
  1674	   531	   177	  `core_ibex_fcov_if.sv`'s `cp_controller_fsm`/`cp_controller_fsm_sleep`
  1675	   532	   178	  covergroups fatally errored (`Error-[FCIBH] Illegal bin hit`) on legal FSM
  1676	   533	   179	  self-loops (e.g. `RESET=>RESET`) because VCS treats every unlisted
  1677	   534	   180	  transition as illegal rather than the empty set upstream intended. Fixed
  1678	   535	   181	  by guarding the `illegal_bins ... = default sequence;` blocks behind
  1679	   536	   182	  `` `ifndef FCOV_NO_DEFAULT_SEQUENCE ``, with that macro defined only in
  1680	   537	   183	  the `vcs` tool's compile command in
  1681	   538	   184	  `dv/uvm/core_ibex/yaml/rtl_simulation.yaml` (commit `dbba358f`) — no other
  1682	   539	   185	  simulator's build is affected. A real fix requires enumerating the FSM's
  1683	   540	   186	  legal transitions explicitly instead of relying on `default sequence`;
  1684	   541	   187	  out of scope here.
  1685	   542	   188	- **`IBEX_CFG_*` define-name mismatch (fixed, commit `54e01775`).** The TB
  1686	   543	   189	  (`dv/uvm/core_ibex/tb/core_ibex_tb_top.sv`) previously guarded on
  1687	   544	   190	  `IBEX_CFG_BASE_ISA`/`IBEX_CFG_REG_FILE` while `util/ibex_config.py` emits
  1688	   545	   191	  `+define+IBEX_CFG_BaseIsa`/`+define+IBEX_CFG_RegFile` — the spelling
  1689	   546	   192	  mismatch meant the `` `ifdef ``s never matched, so every config silently
  1690	   547	   193	  elaborated with the TB's own CHERIoT-capable defaults regardless of
  1691	   548	   194	  `IBEX_CONFIG`. `RV32ZC` had no TB forwarding at all. All three are fixed;
  1692	   549	   195	  the TB now prints a `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=... RegFile=... RV32ZC=...`
  1693	   550	   196	  banner at time 0 as permanent runtime evidence that a given config
  1694	   551	   197	  actually reached elaboration — e.g. `opentitan` prints
  1695	   552	   198	  `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=BaseIsaRV32IorCHERIoT RegFile=RegFileFF
  1696	   553	   199	  RV32ZC=RV32ZcaZcbZcmp`, `small` prints
  1697	   554	   200	  `TB-CONFIG: BaseIsa=BaseIsaRV32I RegFile=RegFileFF RV32ZC=RV32Zca`
  1698	   555	   201	  (see `docs/dv/evidence/ws1-smoke-regr-note.txt`).
  1699	   556	   202	- **Direct `scripts/*.py` invocation needs `PYTHONPATH`.** Running any
  1700	   557	   203	  `dv/uvm/core_ibex/scripts/*.py` script directly (bypassing `make`) needs
  1701	   558	   204	  `PYTHONPATH` set via `scripts.setup_imports.get_pythonpath()` first — the
  1702	   559	   205	  Makefile does this automatically for every target; a bare
  1703	   560	   206	  `python3 scripts/metadata.py --help` will fail with
  1704	   561	   207	  `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'ibex_config'` otherwise.
  1705	   562

  1709	   566	Unloading: git version 2.26.2
  1710	   567	Unloading: Bender version 0.28.1-tenstorrent8-mr_fixes
  1711	   568	Unloading: Python version 3.9
  1712	   569	Unloading: Perl version perl-5.36.0
  1713	   570	Loading:   soc_tools version 1.0
  1714	   571	Loading:   git version 2.26.2
  1715	   572	Loading:   Bender version 0.28.1-tenstorrent8-mr_fixes
  1716	   573	Loading:   Python version 3.9
  1717	   574	Loading:   Perl version perl-5.36.0
