  1719	   576	     1	# Ibex DV principles
  1720	   577	     2	
  1721	   578	     3	Recurring verification best-practices for this repo — the centralized "how we verify" reference
  1722	   579	     4	for humans and agents. It binds hand-written and generated DV equally: tests, TB components,
  1723	   580	     5	checkers, coverage, and the tooling around them. Flow commands live in
  1724	   581	     6	[`BUILD_AND_SIM.md`](BUILD_AND_SIM.md); cosim architecture in [`COSIM.md`](COSIM.md); the
  1725	   582	     7	generated-test interface contract in `TB_CONTRACT.md` (once WS2 lands).
  1726	   583	     8	
  1727	   584	     9	## 1. Stimulus & drive
  1728	   585	    10	
  1729	   586	    11	- **Realistic drive at the DUT boundary.** Drive ibex the way a real system does: instructions
  1730	   587	    12	  through generated programs (riscv-dv or directed assembly) fetched over the instruction bus,
  1731	   588	    13	  data through the memory interface agents, interrupts through the irq lines. A backdoor or
  1732	   589	    14	  force is acceptable only where the boundary itself is the thing being modeled (e.g. memory
  1733	   590	    15	  model contents) — never as a shortcut around a DUT input you could drive for real. A forced
  1734	   591	    16	  internal net can silently no-op under VCS visibility rules; if a backdoor is unavoidable,
  1735	   592	    17	  verify it took (write → clock → read back → error on mismatch).
  1736	   593	    18	- **If it's a config, program it.** CSR-controlled behavior is set through real CSR-writing
  1737	   594	    19	  instructions in the test program, not by poking DUT state. TB-parameter behavior is set
  1738	   595	    20	  through the config system (`IBEX_CONFIG`, `+define+IBEX_CFG_*`) — and the `TB-CONFIG:` banner
  1739	   596	    21	  exists so every log proves which configuration actually elaborated.
  1740	   597	    22	- **Randomize, don't walk.** A test explores its state space to catch corners, not to confirm
  1741	   598	    23	  one path. Randomly select stimulus per iteration with distribution weights; pin a directed
  1742	   599	    24	  iteration inside the random stream only to guarantee a specific corner. Never reduce a test
  1743	   600	    25	  to a single directed case "because the answer is known".
  1744	   601	    26	- **Future-proof every count.** Derive counts and ranges from their canonical parameter
  1745	   602	    27	  (`ibex_pkg` enums/parameters, config fields), never a re-typed literal — when the design
  1746	   603	    28	  parameter changes, the stimulus must follow with no edit.
  1747	   604	    29	- **Cover the transition, not just the steady state.** Interleave alternative modes (e.g.
  1748	   605	    30	  interrupt on/off phases, debug entries, PMP region reconfiguration) so the switch itself is
  1749	   606	    31	  exercised, not just each mode in isolation.
  1750	   607	    32	- **Own your preconditions.** A test programs the machine state its behavior depends on
  1751	   608	    33	  (privilege mode, MIE/MSTATUS, PMP setup) explicitly at entry and assumes nothing about
  1752	   609	    34	  incoming state; on observing unexpected state, error rather than adapt silently.
  1753	   610	    35	- **Sensitizing operands.** Distinct, non-degenerate data; interpretation-distinct values so a
  1754	   611	    36	  wrong interpretation is observable. No all-zeros or identity values that mask a fault.
  1755	   612	    37	
  1756	   613	    38	## 2. Checking & correctness
  1757	   614	    39	
  1758	   615	    40	- **Check at the interface boundary; internal signals only when unavoidable.** Prefer checks at
  1759	   616	    41	  module/bus boundaries and architectural state (the cosim comparison is the model: retired
  1760	   617	    42	  instructions, RF writes, PC). An internal-signal check is fragile to design change and does
  1761	   618	    43	  not show end-to-end behavior — when one is unavoidable, make its failure message identify
  1762	   619	    44	  itself ("checked signal moved/renamed", not a phantom bug) and pair it with an end-to-end
  1763	   620	    45	  check.
  1764	   621	    46	- **Deterministic ⇒ derive from intent.** A reference model computes expected values from the
  1765	   622	    47	  ISA/spec and the driven stimulus — never by copying an RTL decode net. A model mirrored from
  1766	   623	    48	  the RTL only proves the RTL matches itself. Reach for an internal anchor only when the
  1767	   624	    49	  quantity's starting state is genuinely unknowable from intent, and document it.
  1768	   625	    50	- **End-to-end round-trip.** Use data again through the consumer's output (store then load
  1769	   626	    51	  back; CSR write then read). Handing data off correctly does not prove it was applied.
  1770	   627	    52	- **Place the check where the failure lands — never before it.** A check that passes before
  1771	   628	    53	  full propagation is a false pass waiting to happen; gate on the commit/settle event.
  1772	   629	    54	- **Verify the result of every transaction — including the "harmless" ones.** A read issued
  1773	   630	    55	  only to observe state must still assert both that it landed and that the value matches; a
  1774	   631	    56	  dropped or perturbing read otherwise passes unnoticed.
  1775	   632	    57	- **Fail through a mechanism the flow actually collects.** In the SV-UVM flow, that is
  1776	   633	    58	  `uvm_error`/`uvm_fatal` (scanned by `scripts/check_logs.py` into `trr.yaml`/`regr.log`); in
  1777	   634	    59	  cocotb tests, a Python `assert`/raised exception fails the cocotb test which fails the run. A
  1778	   635	    60	  bare `$display`/log line fails nothing. Know your failure path and prove it once (see §6).
  1779	   636	    61	- **Predict-and-check.** Per-feature expectations computed from randomized stimulus + sampled
  1780	   637	    62	  config, compared against observation. Keep predictors pure and host-testable where possible.
  1781	   638	    63	- **Ground-truth at the waveform for new checkers.** Confirm a new monitor/checker against the
  1782	   639	    64	  actual wave at the cycles of interest at least once — it catches mis-reads (wrong hierarchical
  1783	   640	    65	  handle, packed-signal misinterpretation, silent-idle None handle) that a green test hides.
  1784	   641	    66	
  1785	   642	    67	## 3. Scoreboard & infrastructure
  1786	   643	    68	
  1787	   644	    69	- **Passive, always-on checking is the default.** The best check is one a test author cannot
  1788	   645	    70	  forget: derived automatically, applied on every transaction in every test (the cosim referee
  1789	   646	    71	  is the archetype). Active in-test checks exist for (a) "did the targeted scenario actually
  1790	   647	    72	  fire" — the reason the test exists — and (b) checks unique or too costly to run passively.
  1791	   648	    73	  Both layers are needed; a passive floor does not remove a test's duty to check its own
  1792	   649	    74	  driven data and effects.
  1793	   650	    75	- **Always-true invariants live in shared infrastructure; "did it fire" lives in the test.**
  1794	   651	    76	- **No-modify reuse of shared/vendored infra.** riscv-dv and lowrisc_ip are vendored: extend
  1795	   652	    77	  through the extension points (`riscv_dv_extension/`, TB hooks), don't patch vendored code
  1796	   653	    78	  ad-hoc. When a genuine vendor patch is required it goes through `vendor/patches/` with the
  1797	   654	    79	  vendoring flow, never a direct edit.
  1798	   655	    80	
  1799	   656	    81	## 4. RTL truth & honesty
  1800	   657	    82	
  1801	   658	    83	- **Ground in intent, log divergence.** Models and testplans derive from the ISA spec and
  1802	   659	    84	  documented intent. When RTL and intent disagree: if the RTL is more complete/correct, RTL
  1803	   660	    85	  wins and the disagreement is logged; if the RTL is less complete than the documented intent,
  1804	   661	    86	  that is a latent-bug candidate — write the test to the intended behavior, mark it as a known
  1805	   662	    87	  expected-fail, and drive the fix. DV does not modify RTL to make tests pass.
  1806	   663	    88	- **Don't hide failures.** Never fake-pass, silently down-scope, or paper over something that
  1807	   664	    89	  cannot be driven or checked; surface limitations explicitly (the FCIBH workaround in
  1808	   665	    90	  `BUILD_AND_SIM.md` is the pattern: documented, scoped, with the follow-up named).
  1809	   666	    91	- **Evidence over inference.** If a question is answerable from waves, RTL, docs, git history,
  1810	   667	    92	  or a quick experiment — check it. State unverified claims as unverified. A mechanism you
  1811	   668	    93	  explained but did not observe is a guess.
  1812	   669	    94	- **FCOV: build samplable, prune impossible.** A coverpoint may be built before stimulus can
  1813	   670	    95	  hit it (0% marks intent), but genuinely unhittable bins are pruned/`ignore_bins`'d so
  1814	   671	    96	  coverage isn't diluted. No duplicate coverpoints — extend, don't re-cover. Beware simulator
  1815	   672	    97	  semantics: `illegal_bins = default sequence` is not portable (see the FCIBH gotcha).
  1816	   673	    98	
  1817	   674	    99	## 5. Tooling & hygiene
  1818	   675	   100	
  1819	   676	   101	- **Toggleable debug, never unconditional prints.** Debug output gates behind a plusarg;
  1820	   677	   102	  plusarg names are declared once (test/TB constants), not scattered string literals — a
  1821	   678	   103	  mistyped gate silently no-ops.
  1822	   679	   104	- **Concise diagnostics.** A failure message states its triggering condition — id plus
  1823	   680	   105	  expected-vs-actual — nothing more. Root-cause narration belongs in docs, not runtime messages.
  1824	   681	   106	- **Self-sufficient docs and code.** Every doc, commit message, comment, and identifier is
  1825	   682	   107	  resolvable on its own: expand a tag/ID on first use or link the defining doc. Code never
  1826	   683	   108	  depends on a bare cross-file positional index or opaque tag; bind by named import or stable
  1827	   684	   109	  key that fails loud on reorder.
  1828	   685	   110	- **Concise code comments — intent only.** What and why, never history ("moved from X",
  1829	   686	   111	  "previously Y") — that lives in git.
  1830	   687	   112	- **Single source of truth.** A value meaning the same thing in two places is defined once and
  1831	   688	   113	  imported: `ibex_pkg` parameters, config fields, one constants home per language domain.
  1832	   689	   114	- **No hardcoded paths — filesystem or hierarchy.** Filesystem paths anchor to the repo root
  1833	   690	   115	  or `ci/env.sh`-exported variables. Hierarchical RTL references (SV bind paths, cocotb
  1834	   691	   116	  handles) resolve through one central place per domain (the fcov binds; the cocotb common
  1835	   692	   117	  package), so a rename fails loudly there instead of silently per call-site.
  1836	   693	   118	- **Site realities** (see `BUILD_AND_SIM.md` gotchas): the module command exits nonzero on
  1837	   694	   119	  success; background notifications are unreliable — poll artifacts with deadlines and watchdog
  1838	   695	   120	  long-running work; clear `PYTHONPATH` around pip operations.
  1839	   696	   121	
  1840	   697	   122	## 6. Trust & evidence
  1841	   698	   123	
  1842	   699	   124	Every claim about verification is backed by committed evidence — logs, reports, banners — not
  1843	   700	   125	prose. The `TB-CONFIG:` banner, the evidence files under `docs/dv/evidence/`, and the review
  1844	   701	   126	artifacts under `docs/dv/reviews/` are the standing examples.
  1845	   702	   127	
  1846	   703	   128	<!-- TRUST-TRIAD-CANONICAL-BEGIN -->
  1847	   704	   129	**The trust triad** — required for every new test, checker, assertion, or covergroup, whether
  1848	   705	   130	human-written or generated:
  1849	   706	   131	
  1850	   707	   132	1. **TDD** — the behavior is specified by a failing check before the implementation that makes
  1851	   708	   133	   it pass; the red→green transcript is part of the evidence.
  1852	   709	   134	2. **Mutation-proof** — a new checker or assertion counts only when a named mutation (recorded
  1853	   710	   135	   as id, file:line, original, mutated, expected detector) is caught by the NAMED checker with
  1854	   711	   136	   hidden referees inert, plus a checker-ablation negative control (checker disabled ⇒ the
  1855	   712	   137	   mutation survives). "Hidden referees inert" operationally: run with `+disable_cosim=1` (cosim
  1856	   713	   138	   mismatches become informational and cannot fail the test) and verify the failure signature in
  1857	   714	   139	   the log belongs to the named checker.
  1858	   715	   140	3. **fcov-expectation** — every new test declares the functional-coverage bins it intends to
  1859	   716	   141	   hit; declared-but-unhit bins FAIL the run. Verification is per-test and pre-merge (merged
  1860	   717	   142	   databases let one test claim another's bins), generated covergroups live in an isolated
  1861	   718	   143	   namespace, and every sampling condition gets an anti-vacuity review (a bin hit by an
  1862	   719	   144	   always-true sample proves nothing).
  1863	   720	   145	<!-- TRUST-TRIAD-CANONICAL-END -->
  1864	   721	   146	
  1865	   722	   147	- **Self-proving checks.** The triad's rules 2 and 3 generalize: a check never exercised to
  1866	   723	   148	  fail is not trusted; a stimulus never proven to arrive is not trusted.
  1867	   724	   149	- **Reviewer independence.** The executing model never self-approves — the cross-model review
  1868	   725	   150	  policy in `CLAUDE.md` governs; artifacts live under `docs/dv/reviews/`.
  1869	   726	   151	- **No hidden-referee reliance.** Generated DV must carry its own checking and may not lean on
  1870	   727	   152	  referees it cannot see (the cosim demotion rules and the checking obligation live in the
  1871	   728	   153	  spec's fence workstream and `TB_CONTRACT.md` once WS2 lands).
  1872	   729	
  1873	   730	exec

_TestEntry = Dict[str, object]
_TestEntries = List[_TestEntry]


@typechecked
def filter_tests_by_config(cfg: ibex_config.Config,
                           test_list: _TestEntries) -> _TestEntries:
    """Filter out any unsupported tests from being executed.

    e.g. if the "small" config has been specified, this function will filter
    out all tests that require B-extension and PMP parameters

    This function will parse the set of RTL parameters required by a given
    test (if any) and ensure that those parameters are supported by the
    selected core config.

    Doing this allows the run flow to be smarter about running regressions
    with different configs (useful for CI flows).

    Arguments:
        cfg: ibex_config.Config object of built system
        test_list: list of test entry objects parsed from the YAML testlist

    Returns:
        filtered_test_list: a list of test entry objects, filtered such that
                            all tests incompatible with the specified ibex
                            config have been removed.
    """
    filtered_test_list = []

    for test in test_list:
        if "rtl_params" not in test:
            # We currently only exclude tests by mismatching 'rtl_params', so
            # if that key is missing then the test is accepted by default.
            filtered_test_list.append(test)
        else:
            param_dict = test['rtl_params']
            assert isinstance(param_dict, dict)
            for p, p_val in param_dict.items():
                # Parameters are strings or ints, or lists of those two. Coerce
                # to the latter to make the code below simpler.
                if isinstance(p_val, str) or isinstance(p_val, int):
                    p_val = [p_val]

                config_val = cfg.params.get(p, None)

                # Throw an error if required RTL parameters in the testlist
                # have been formatted incorrectly (typos, wrong parameters,
                # etc)
                if config_val is None:
                    raise ValueError('Parameter {} not found in config {}'
                                     .format(p, cfg))

                # Ibex has some enum parameters, so as a result some tests are
                # able to run with several of these parameter values (like
                # bitmanipulation tests). If this is the case, the testlist
                # will specify all legal enum values, check if any of them
                # match the config.
                if config_val not in p_val:
                    logger.warning(
                        f"Rejecting test: {test['test']}. It specifies "
                        f"rtl_params of {p_val}, which doesn't contain the "
                        f"expected '{config_val}'.")
                    break

                # The test is accepted if we got this far
                filtered_test_list.append(test)

    return filtered_test_list
        return md

    @classmethod
    @typechecked
    def construct_from_metadata_dir(cls, dir_metadata: pathlib.Path):
        """Construct metadata object from exported object using default filenames."""
        md_pickle = pathlib.Path(dir_metadata)/'metadata.pickle'
        md = cls.construct_from_pickle(md_pickle)
        return md

    def get_tests_and_counts(self) -> List[Tuple[Tuple[str, int, TestType], object]]:
        """Get a list of tests and the number of iterations to run of each.

        ibex_config should be the name of the Ibex configuration to be tested.

        If test is provided, it gives the test or tests (as a comma separated
        string) to narrow to. Use the special name "all" to run all the tests.

        If iterations is provided, it should be a positive number and overrides the
        number of iterations for each test.
        """
        riscvdv_matched_list: ibex_cmd._TestEntries = self.process_riscvdv_testlist()
        directed_matched_list: ibex_cmd._TestEntries = self.process_directed_testlist()

        if not (riscvdv_matched_list or directed_matched_list):
            raise RuntimeError("No matching tests found in testlists.")

        # Filter tests by the chosen ibex configuration
        riscvdv_filtered_list = ibex_cmd.filter_tests_by_config(
            ibex_config.parse_config(self.ibex_config, str(self.ibex_configs)),
            riscvdv_matched_list)
        directed_filtered_list = ibex_cmd.filter_tests_by_config(
            ibex_config.parse_config(self.ibex_config, str(self.ibex_configs)),
            directed_matched_list)

        # Convert to desired output format (and check for well-formedness)
        ret = []
        for test in riscvdv_filtered_list:
            name, iterations = (test['test'], test['iterations'])
            assert isinstance(name, str) and isinstance(iterations, int) \
                                         and iterations > 0
            ret.append(((name, iterations, TestType.RISCVDV), test))
        for test in directed_filtered_list:
            name, iterations = (test['test'], test['iterations'])
            assert isinstance(name, str) and isinstance(iterations, int) \
                                         and iterations > 0
            ret.append(((name, iterations, TestType.DIRECTED), test))

        return ret

    def process_riscvdv_testlist(self) -> ibex_cmd._TestEntries:
        """Extract test information from the riscvdv testlist yaml."""
        matched_list: ibex_cmd._TestEntries = []

        # Get all the tests from the 'testlist' that match the 'test' argument.
        riscvdv_lib.process_regression_list(
            testlist=self.ibex_riscvdv_testlist,
            test=('all' if any(x in self.test.split(',')
                               for x in ['all_riscvdv', 'all']) else
                  self.test),
            iterations=(self.iterations or 0),

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Environment entry point for ibex auto-DV work. Source, don't execute:
#   source ci/env.sh
# Central authority for tool paths — nothing else in the repo hardcodes them.
#
# Contract: supported on site hosts that already carry the standard site
# profile — a login shell (`bash -l`) or any shell that inherits the site
# environment (Modules init, PATH, etc.) from one. This script loads
# additional site Modules on top of that; it does not bootstrap a sanitized
# shell that never sourced the site profile at all.

IBEX_CI_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
export IBEX_TOOLS_DIR="${IBEX_TOOLS_DIR:-/localdev/fzhang/ws/tools}"
export IBEX_PYTHON="${IBEX_PYTHON:-/tools_soc/opensrc/python/python-3.12.10/bin/python3}"

# --- Host GCC (vcs/spike DPI compiles need gcc-toolset-11; a non-login shell
# doesn't pick this up from /etc/profile.d) ---
if [ -f /opt/rh/gcc-toolset-11/enable ]; then
    source /opt/rh/gcc-toolset-11/enable
fi

# --- Simulator (VCS must match the Verdi release already on PATH) ---
source /etc/profile.d/modules.sh 2>/dev/null || true
# synopsys/vcs has an undeclared prereq on synopsys/licenses; 2.3 is the newest
# available (of 1.0-2.3) and a superset of older servers, so pick it.
# This site's module command exits 1 even on a successful load, hence `|| true`.
module load synopsys/licenses/2.3 2>/dev/null || true
module load synopsys/vcs/X-2025.06-SP2 2>/dev/null || true
export VERDI_HOME="${VERDI_HOME:-/tools_vendor/synopsys/verdi/X-2025.06-SP2}"

# dtc is a spike build dep (ci/build-spike.sh); this site's module command
# exits 1 even on a successful load, hence the `|| true`.
module load dtc/1.7.2 2>/dev/null || true

# --- GitHub CLI (auth already configured for fzhangTT) ---
export PATH="/tools_vendor/FOSS/gh/2.53.0/bin:$PATH"

# --- RISC-V toolchain (Task 4 decides which; lowRISC preferred) ---
# _IBEX_RV_TC_AUTO caches our own last pick so re-sourcing after installing
# the lowRISC toolchain picks it up, without clobbering a real user override
# (RISCV_TOOLCHAIN set to anything other than our own last pick).
if [ -n "${RISCV_TOOLCHAIN:-}" ] && [ "${RISCV_TOOLCHAIN:-}" != "${_IBEX_RV_TC_AUTO:-}" ]; then
    _ibex_rv_tc="$RISCV_TOOLCHAIN"
elif [ -x "$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/lowrisc-toolchain-gcc-rv32imcb/bin/riscv32-unknown-elf-gcc" ]; then
    _ibex_rv_tc="$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/lowrisc-toolchain-gcc-rv32imcb"
else
    # Site riscv64 multilib fallback (bitmanip tests unavailable)
    _ibex_rv_tc=/tools_risc/opensrc/latest/newlib
fi
export RISCV_TOOLCHAIN="$_ibex_rv_tc"
export _IBEX_RV_TC_AUTO="$_ibex_rv_tc"
unset _ibex_rv_tc

if [ -x "$RISCV_TOOLCHAIN/bin/riscv32-unknown-elf-gcc" ]; then
    export RISCV_GCC="$RISCV_TOOLCHAIN/bin/riscv32-unknown-elf-gcc"
    export RISCV_OBJCOPY="$RISCV_TOOLCHAIN/bin/riscv32-unknown-elf-objcopy"
else
    export RISCV_GCC="$RISCV_TOOLCHAIN/bin/riscv64-unknown-elf-gcc"
    export RISCV_OBJCOPY="$RISCV_TOOLCHAIN/bin/riscv64-unknown-elf-objcopy"
fi

# --- Spike (lowRISC ibex_cosim fork, built by ci/build-spike.sh) ---
export SPIKE_INSTALL="$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/spike-ibex-cosim"
export SPIKE_PATH="$SPIKE_INSTALL/bin"
export PKG_CONFIG_PATH="$SPIKE_INSTALL/lib/pkgconfig${PKG_CONFIG_PATH:+:$PKG_CONFIG_PATH}"

# --- Python venv (created by Task 2; optional until then) ---
if [ -f "$IBEX_CI_ROOT/.venv/bin/activate" ]; then
    source "$IBEX_CI_ROOT/.venv/bin/activate"
fi

echo "ibex env: vcs=$(command -v vcs || echo MISSING)" \
     "gcc=$(command -v "$RISCV_GCC" >/dev/null && echo "$RISCV_GCC" || echo MISSING)" \
     "spike=$([ -d "$SPIKE_INSTALL" ] && echo "$SPIKE_INSTALL" || echo NOT-BUILT)"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Build the lowRISC spike fork the ibex cosim TB links against.
# Rev pinned to match this repo's flake.nix; dv/cosim/* is written against it.
# Requires dtc on PATH — loaded by ci/env.sh.
set -euo pipefail
SPIKE_REV=4b97396656485a129119deaec2ba35e5bf354841
IBEX_TOOLS_DIR="${IBEX_TOOLS_DIR:-/localdev/fzhang/ws/tools}"
PREFIX="${SPIKE_INSTALL:-$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/spike-ibex-cosim}"
SRC="$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/src/riscv-isa-sim-lowrisc"

mkdir -p "$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/src"
if [ ! -d "$SRC/.git" ]; then
    git clone https://github.com/lowRISC/riscv-isa-sim.git "$SRC"
fi
git -C "$SRC" fetch --all --tags
git -C "$SRC" checkout "$SPIKE_REV"

mkdir -p "$SRC/build"
cd "$SRC/build"
# Static gcc runtimes for spike's own objects; note DT_NEEDED on libstdc++.so.6
# remains via shared Boost. Runtime compatibility on this site rests on
# gcc-toolset-11's DTS model (symbol floor = RHEL8 system libstdc++); the
# definitive check is the TB DPI link + simv load in the smoke test.
../configure --enable-commitlog --enable-misaligned --prefix="$PREFIX" \
             LDFLAGS="-static-libstdc++ -static-libgcc"
make -j"$(nproc)"
make install
echo "spike installed to $PREFIX"
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Fetch the lowRISC rv32imcb toolchain release (bitmanip-patched GCC).
# Version matches ci/vars.env (RISCV_TOOLCHAIN_TAR_VERSION=20220210-1).
set -euo pipefail
VER=20220210-1
NAME=lowrisc-toolchain-gcc-rv32imcb-$VER
URL=https://github.com/lowRISC/lowrisc-toolchains/releases/download/$VER/$NAME.tar.xz
IBEX_TOOLS_DIR="${IBEX_TOOLS_DIR:-/localdev/fzhang/ws/tools}"
DEST="$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR/lowrisc-toolchain-gcc-rv32imcb"

mkdir -p "$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR"
cd "$IBEX_TOOLS_DIR"
[ -f "$NAME.tar.xz" ] || { curl -fL -o "$NAME.tar.xz.part" "$URL" && mv "$NAME.tar.xz.part" "$NAME.tar.xz"; }
tar xf "$NAME.tar.xz"
rm -rf "$DEST"
mv "$NAME" "$DEST"
"$DEST/bin/riscv32-unknown-elf-gcc" --version | head -1

exec

docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-82-**Cross-model review policy** (in `CLAUDE.md` + `AGENTS.md`; the `cross-review` skill executes it):
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-83-- The executing model never self-approves.
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-84-- **Before execution:** plan/spec/testplan reviewed by the other model — Claude executing ⇒ `codex exec` reviews against the rubrics + `dv_principles.md`; codex executing ⇒ `claude -p` reviews.
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-85-- **After execution:** diff review by the other model — `codex exec review` for the codex direction; `claude -p` + rubrics for the reverse.
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-86-- Review artifacts (verdict + findings) are committed under `docs/dv/reviews/` — this doubles as the challenge's trust-evidence trail. Each artifact records **reviewer identity** (CLI version, model ID, reasoning setting) and the **exact review target** (commit SHAs or explicit diff scope — `codex exec review` invoked with an explicit `--base`/`--commit`/uncommitted target, never an ambient default).
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-87-- The verdict is **machine-readable** (APPROVE / APPROVE-WITH-CHANGES / REQUEST-CHANGES in a fixed header) and **gating**: CI (and the executing agent) must not proceed past a REQUEST-CHANGES without a recorded re-review. Review artifacts live under the fence (WS7) — they discuss DV content.

docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-138-
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-139-**Fenced content — default-deny** (single source of truth: `ci/fence.yaml`). Sensitive roots are denied wholesale; visibility requires an explicit file-level **allowlist entry with a one-line rationale**, and the sync script **rejects any path it cannot classify** (new files must be triaged into the manifest before entering a snapshot). Deny roots and notable cases:
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-140-- `dv/**` — all existing DV: tests, testlists, `riscv_dv_extension/`, `directed_tests/`, `fcov/` (human coverage model), `env/`, `common/`, `dv/cosim/`, verilator/formal collateral. Allowlisted back in: the `dv/auto_dv/**` namespace, and interface-defining files needed to compile against the TB contract (sequence items, virtual interface definitions, hook/API packages — they carry no stimulus/checking/coverage knowledge; where a file mixes both, extract an interface package into an allowed path).
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-141-- Verification-describing documentation: `doc/03_reference/{testplan,coverage_plan,verification,verification_stages,cosim}.rst`, `doc/01_overview/verification_overview.rst` (names tests, describes coverage strategy), TB diagrams (`doc/03_reference/images/tb*.svg`), `dv/uvm/core_ibex/README.md`.
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-142-- `vendor/patches/**` **and their applied results**: the checked-in `vendor/google_riscv-dv/` already has local patches applied (`google_riscv-dv.vendor.hjson`), so the snapshot **replaces it with pristine upstream at the locked revision** (`71666eba`, from the lock file) — or omits it and Zone A imports upstream riscv-dv itself.
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-143-- The lowRISC spike fork (`ibex_cosim` branch, built by WS1) — Zone B equipment only; Zone A may build its own reference-model integration from **upstream** `riscv/riscv-isa-sim`.
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md:144:- **Derived/infra documents that paraphrase fenced knowledge:** `docs/dv/COSIM.md`, `docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md` (names existing tests), `docs/dv/evidence/**`, `docs/dv/reviews/**`, `docs/superpowers/**` (specs/plans discuss existing DV), the full tree's `.mex/**`, `ci/reviews/**` where rubrics reference existing DV (e.g. test-overlap). docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-145-
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-146-**Zone A — clean room (generation).** A published **`cleanroom` branch with orphan snapshot history**: `ci/sync-cleanroom.sh` builds a tree of allowed paths only (from the manifest) and appends it as a snapshot commit (orphan root; no ancestry to `master`), then pushes; it fails if any fenced glob would enter the snapshot. Because no commit in the branch's history ever contained a fenced blob, a clean clone is airtight:
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-147-
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-148-```
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-149-git clone --single-branch --branch cleanroom https://github.com/fzhangTT/ibex.git ibex-cleanroom
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-150-```
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-151-
docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-01-auto-dv-setup-design.md-152-`--single-branch` fetches only that branch's objects — fenced blobs are absent from the clone entirely, not merely hidden. Updates are plain `git pull` (append-only snapshots, no force-push). **User entry point:** `ci/make-cleanroom.sh` automates the whole thing from a freshly pulled `master` — it performs the single-branch clone into a sibling dir (default `../ibex-cleanroom`, configurable), verifies zero fenced files anywhere in the clone (history included), and leaves the user checked out on `cleanroom` ready to `git checkout -b cleanroom/<topic>`. It is deliberately a **separate clone, never `git worktree`** — worktrees share the parent's object store, so fenced blobs would remain retrievable. Note `cleanroom` reflects `master` as of the last sync (Jenkins job or manual `ci/sync-cleanroom.sh`). **Sync integrity:** syncs are serialized (lock; the branch ref is protected against non-sync pushes), each snapshot commit records the source `master` SHA + the manifest digest in its message, and every sync audits the *entire existing snapshot history* against the current manifest — not just the new tree. **Revocation:** if a leak is found or a file is reclassified as fenced, append-only history is abandoned — the sync generates a new orphan root (`cleanroom` generation N+1, force-rotated), and Zone B/landing reject work based on retired generations, forcing fresh clones. Generation work happens on branches off `cleanroom`; `ci/land-from-cleanroom.sh` lands finished work onto a `master`-based branch for normal PR review, and the landing validator checks ancestry from an approved snapshot and confines changed paths to `dv/auto_dv/**` (no symlinks, renames, or deletes outside it). A sparse-checkout script (`ci/make-cleanroom-sparse.sh`) is provided as a *human convenience only*, documented with its caveat: the local object store still contains fenced history, so it is a view, not a boundary.

### Task 4: Workflow skills — `sim-debug`, `regress`, `lint-check`, `dv-principles-check`

**Files:** Create `.claude/skills/{sim-debug,regress,lint-check,dv-principles-check}/SKILL.md`

**Interfaces:**
- `sim-debug`: input = test name or out-dir; procedure = locate newest `out*/run/tests/<t>.<s>/`, classify failure by stage using WS1's verified log names (gen → `compile.riscvdv.log` (0 bytes = clean) → `compile_tb_stdstreams.log` → `rtl_sim_stdstreams.log`/`rtl_sim.log` → `trr.yaml`), extract the minimal excerpt, check for cosim mismatch signatures (from `docs/dv/COSIM.md` §2), FCIBH note, watchdog `$fatal` note (cocotb). Output contract: stage, excerpt, hypothesis, next probe.
- `regress`: wraps the BUILD_AND_SIM.md commands directly (single test / TEST=all / COV=1), reminds fresh-OUT/metadata rules, reads `regr.log`/`report.html`/`regr_junit.xml`, summarizes pass rates + failure buckets by first-error signature. Carries an explicit maintenance note: WS4 will deliver `ci/jenkins/*.sh`; this skill MUST be updated to wrap those when they land (the note is part of the skill text, so the follow-up is discoverable).
- `lint-check`: discover and run the repo's actual lint entry points (inspect `lint/` and root Makefile targets — verible/verilator-lint; document exactly what exists and works TODAY; if a target is broken on this site, the skill says so rather than pretending).
- `dv-principles-check`: conformance review of a named diff/file against `docs/dv/dv_principles.md` §1-§6 (explicitly NOT a general code review; cites section numbers; never duplicates the doc).

- [ ] Step 1: write the four skills (frontmatter name/description per Claude Code format; procedures reference real paths/commands only).
- [ ] Step 2 (live test): run `sim-debug` mentally-executable check — feed it the WS1 FCIBH failure artifacts (they're described in `docs/dv/process-logs/ws1/task-7-report.md`): walking its procedure must reach "fcov illegal-bin fatal at ~2us" from the log names alone; adjust the skill if the procedure dead-ends. For `lint-check`, actually run the discovered lint target once and record the result.
- [ ] Step 3: commit (`[ai] Add DV workflow skills (sim-debug, regress, lint-check, principles-check)`).

### Task 5: Trust skills + `simple-english` + agents

**Files:**
- Create: `.claude/skills/mutation-check/SKILL.md` (+ `mutations/README.md` seed format), `.claude/skills/fcov-expectation/SKILL.md` (+ `ci/check_fcov_expectations.py`), `.claude/skills/simple-english/` (vendored port with LICENSE + references/, rescoped), `.claude/agents/ibex-debug-analyzer.md`, `.claude/agents/ibex-test-generator.md`

**Interfaces:**
- `mutation-check`: procedure = pick/declare the mutation (one-line RTL/TB change, recorded in `dv/auto_dv/mutations/` format: id, file:line, original, mutated, expected detector), apply, run the covering test **with hidden referees inert** — operational definition: `+disable_cosim=1` on the sim (downgrades cosim mismatches to informational so they CANNOT fail the test) plus verification that the failure signature in the log belongs to the NAMED checker — confirm the named checker fires, ablation control (checker disabled ⇒ mutation survives), revert, commit the evidence record. No "where applicable" softening: every mutation-proof follows this procedure.
- `fcov-expectation`: manifest format `<test>.fcov.yaml` (list of covergroup.bin names, generated covergroups in an isolated namespace, each bin's sampling condition anti-vacuity-reviewed — the skill text carries both requirements); `ci/check_fcov_expectations.py` queries PER-TEST coverage isolated by `-cm_name` (the flow writes one shared `shared_cov/test.vdb` differentiated per test — prove the per-test query works, e.g. urg `-show tests`/test-attributed report, and document the exact mechanism); **wired into the flow's check stage**: extend the check path (`check_logs.py`/`TestRunResult` or the `ibex_sim.mk:59`-region check rule) so a discovered manifest triggers the query and an unhit declaration records a distinct failure mode in `trr.yaml` → the test FAILS in `regr.log`.
- Agents: debug-analyzer = sim-debug procedure + COSIM.md knowledge + escalation rules; test-generator = mandates TDD + mutation-check + fcov-expectation (embeds the canonical triad block verbatim), knows the three test shapes (riscv-dv testlist entry, directed test, cocotb test per TB_CONTRACT.md), carries the site gotchas (poll artifacts, watchdogs).

- [ ] Step 1: write `check_fcov_expectations.py` with a self-test mode (`--self-test` builds a tiny fake report + manifest, checks hit and unhit paths both behave) and run it green. Then validate against REAL data: run one live single-test `COV=1` sim (fresh OUT — commands from BUILD_AND_SIM.md, ~2-3 min), generate a bin-detail per-test report from its coverage DB via the proven `-cm_name` isolation mechanism, and show one known-hit and one known-unhit bin classified correctly; commit that bin-detail report as the fixture (`docs/dv/evidence/ws3-fcov-fixture/`). (The committed WS1 `dashboard.txt` has NO bin-level data — do not use it for this.)
- [ ] Step 2: wire the checker into the check stage per the interface above; regression proof: craft a temporary manifest declaring a bin the live test provably does not hit, rerun the check stage, and show the test FAILS in `regr.log` with the distinct failure mode in `trr.yaml`; then remove the temporary manifest. Transcript in report.
- [ ] Step 2b: write `mutation-check` + seed README; **live-fire it once** on this task's own deliverable: declare and apply a mutation to `check_fcov_expectations.py`'s hit/unhit comparison (record: id, file:line, original, mutated, expected detector = `--self-test`), run the self-test, confirm it FAILS on the mutated code and passes after revert. Real caught-mutation transcript in the report.
- [ ] Step 3: port `simple-english` (keep vendored LICENSE + references; rescope the in-scope surfaces to: `docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md`, `docs/dv/TB_CONTRACT.md`, `docs/dv/FENCE.md`, skill descriptions).
- [ ] Step 4: write the two agents.
- [ ] Step 5: commit (`[ai] Add trust skills (mutation-check, fcov-expectation), simple-english, agents`).

### Task 6: Validation gate — both models, end to end

**Files:** Evidence only (`docs/dv/evidence/ws3-validation.md`); possible small fixes to earlier files, individually committed.

- [ ] Step 1: `python3 .codex/compat/validator.py --repo-root .` → green in the working tree, AND from a temporary clean clone/archive of the branch (`git archive` to a temp dir or a scratch clone) → green there too (catches dangling symlinks and untracked-file dependencies). Fix + commit anything caught.
- [ ] Step 2: validator `--probe` mode: live codex launches from repo root and one subdirectory assert the effective instructions loaded; record output.
- [ ] Step 3: Claude side — a dispatched subagent invokes one skill (`regress` on the committed WS1 evidence) end-to-end and its output matches the skill's contract; record the transcript summary.
- [ ] Step 4: codex side — codex EXERCISES a skill end-to-end, not just lists it: `command codex exec --sandbox read-only "Follow the dv-principles-check skill (discover it via .agents/skills/shared) against docs/dv/BUILD_AND_SIM.md as the review target; produce its contracted output format"` → verify the output is the skill's contracted format with real §-citations; record it.
- [ ] Step 5: cross-review full-cycle closure on a real change: the pre-execution artifact for WS3 already exists (`docs/dv/reviews/2026-09-01-codex-review-03-ws2-ws3-plans.md`); now run the `cross-review` skill in diff mode over the WS3 commit range as the post-execution review of the SAME change, gate on its verdict per the policy (stop/remediate/re-review on REQUEST-CHANGES), and commit the artifact — pre + post artifacts tied to one change completes the demonstrated cycle.
- [ ] Step 6: write + commit the evidence note; update `CLAUDE.md`'s skills index to the final skill list (`[docs] WS3 validation evidence + skills index`).
